## 2026-10-10 · Critic checks the hook; critic@2 and content-writer@2 [M2-12a]
- Context: in the G1 pilot a general statement passed as an English hook with a quality score of 4.37; only the owner's read caught it.
- Decision: `critic@2` (`prompts/src/critic/v2.ts`, built on v1: same input and rendering, the rules gain a **Hook** check) gives a separate 1–5 `scores.hook` (2 or less = a general statement or a title) and, for a weak hook, an issue of the new category `HOOK` on path `hook` whose `suggestedFix` is a concrete replacement line. `content-writer@2` adds one rule: the hook must give a concrete reason to read on. `decideVerdict` (`policy.ts`): a hook scored ≤ 2 (`WEAK_HOOK_SCORE`) asks for a rewrite only when a HOOK issue carries a non-empty fix, which goes into the rewrite instructions; without a fix the verdict is FLAG_FOR_HUMAN ("the hook is weak and the critic gave no concrete fix"), because a rewrite with no instruction would only say "better". The hook score weighs 1 in the quality score and is skipped when absent (critic@1 reports, `CriticReport.scores.hook` is optional). The pipeline reads the answer as `critic.CriticAnswer` (either version). Active versions in `ai/config.ts` are now critic 2 and content-writer 2; `PIPELINE_VERSION` 1.1.0.
- Evidence / links: `policy.test.ts` (weak hook with and without a fix, hook 3 passes, critic@1 answers unaffected, after the last rewrite), `critic/v2.test.ts`, `content-writer/v2.test.ts`.
- Not done: the **live** check that critic@2 flags the pilot's weak hook and does not regress on the synthetic set needs a paid run (`pnpm eval --provider live`; the pilot text is the chef's IP and is not in git). Run it with the next G1 pilot stage; the fake provider cannot judge a hook.
- Impact on plan: M2-12a is ticked on the strength of the code and the policy tests; the live eval is a G1 step.

## 2026-10-10 · Visual director and the visual step of the pipeline [M3-02]
- Decision: `visual-director@1` (`prompts/src/visual-director`) turns a finished draft into a `VisualBrief` (one entry per image slot: GENERATE with a prompt and a negative prompt, LIBRARY with an id, or NONE for an optional slot). The visual DNA of 07 §7.6.4 is in the prompt. Checks in `content/visual/validate.ts`: required slots covered (not NONE), slides and slots exist, no duplicates, a prompt for GENERATE (a mention of text, logo or packaging in it is a MAJOR note), library ids only from the offered candidates, style and hypothesis ids real, aspect equal to the slot's (MINOR; `8:5` slots accept any). The step runs in `generateVariants` after the critic loop, one market after another (the later brief sees the earlier ones as siblings), as a **soft** stage: a failed or invalid brief leaves `visual_brief_json` empty and does not fail the draft (M3-05 reports the missing pictures as `VISUAL_MISSING`; the review screen can plan again). `regenerateVisualBrief` (service + action, roles owner/editor/chef) redoes one variant's brief inline and keeps the old one when the answer is unusable. Library candidates are empty until M3-16. `VISUAL_DIRECTION` joins `generation_config.stages`.
- Impact on plan: none.

## 2026-10-10 · Image provider; V-19 (image part) verified [M3-03, V-19]
- V-19 results (developers.openai.com, 2026-10-10): current image models `gpt-image-2.5-sunburst` (editing precision) and `gpt-image-2.5-flare` (fast, high-quality everyday generation); `POST /v1/images/generations` with `model`, `prompt`, `size` (`1024x1024`, `1536x1024`, `1024x1536`, a custom WIDTHxHEIGHT in multiples of 16, or `auto`), `quality` (`low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`, `auto`), `n`, `output_format` (`png` default, `jpeg`, `webp`), `background`; the answer is `data[].b64_json` plus `usage`; there is no negative prompt; a moderation block is `error.code = "moderation_blocked"`. **Prices could not be read**: the pricing page lists token prices without a per-image table.
- Decision: `lib/src/providers/image/` (`@rc/lib/providers/image`): `ImageProvider.generate({ prompt, negativePrompt?, aspect, label? })` → bytes, mime, size, model, estimated `costUsd`, `params`. OpenAI adapter on plain `fetch`: default model `gpt-image-2.5-flare`, quality `high`, portrait `1024x1536` for 4:5 and 3:4 (cropped to the slot in M3-04), the negative prompt appended as "Avoid: …", retries of 429/5xx/408/timeouts (2), moderation and 4xx are `PermanentError`, no key in errors. **Cost is an estimate** from a price-per-quality table (`DEFAULT_IMAGE_PRICES`: 0.02 / 0.07 / 0.19 / 0.30 / 0.40 USD, the gpt-image-1 portrait figures as a stand-in; `xhigh` and `max` are guesses) that is overridable; replace it with the measured bill in the bake-off (M3-15). Fake: a deterministic gradient PNG per prompt with the label in a `tEXt` chunk, call log, injectable failure. `createImageProvider(env.ai)` picks by `AI_PROVIDER`; wiring into `ServiceContext` comes with the job (M3-05).
- Evidence / links: `lib/src/providers/image/image.test.ts` (MSW contract tests, fake).
- Impact on plan: D-10's "gpt-image family" is now concretely `gpt-image-2.5-flare` by default; `sunburst` is a candidate for the bake-off.

## 2026-10-10 · generate-visual-assets job (J7) [M3-05]
- Decision: `ServiceContext.images` (default refuses, wired in web, jobs and CLI from `AI_PROVIDER`). `generateVisualAssets` (`modules/src/visuals/generate-assets.ts`, job `generate-visual-assets` on the `images` queue, 15 min, started per READY_FOR_REVIEW variant at the end of J5 with key `visuals:{pipelineRunId}:{variantId}`, and by the `generateVisualAssets` action for a retry with `slots`): for each GENERATE entry of the brief, a PENDING `visual_assets` row is claimed first (the unique index makes a concurrent run skip the slot), then provider → `normalizeImage` → original and WebP to storage → READY with phash, size, cost in `params`; a READY asset with the same variant, slide, slot and prompt hash (hash of prompt, negative prompt, aspect and model) is reused, so a rerun pays nothing; a changed prompt makes a new asset. LIBRARY entries point the slot at the library asset id. Three slots run at a time. A failed slot gets a FAILED row and is reported in the result; a provider that is down (`TransientError`) is rethrown after the other slots finish, so the job is retried. `VISUAL_MISSING` is set when any required image slot of any slide is still empty (also when there is no brief) and cleared when none is. The render trigger (J8) is added by M3-12.
- Impact on plan: none.

## 2026-10-10 · Fonts, theme tokens and glyph checks [M3-06]
- Context: brand fonts are not licensed for embedding yet (B-10).
- Decision: OFL fallback bundled in `templates/assets/fonts`: Fraunces (display serif) and Inter (sans), the variable WOFF2 files of the Latin and Latin Extended subsets (about 200 KB), with their licenses. They cover Spanish and English (`ñ ¿ ¡ á é í ó ú ü ° ½ “ ” – —`); **Cyrillic is not bundled** (slides are es-ES and en only; add the Cyrillic subsets if a Russian slide is ever rendered). There are no TTF copies: fontkit reads the WOFF2 files for the glyph check. `@rc/templates/render` (kept out of the main entry because it reads files with `node:fs`): `fontFaceCss(id)` (data-URL `@font-face` with `unicode-range`), `missingGlyphs` / `findMissingGlyphs` (fontkit `hasGlyphForCodePoint`; whitespace ignored; emoji reported; result shaped like `QaReport.missingGlyphs`), `buildTheme(visualSystem, variant)` (an empty `{}` system falls back to `DEFAULT_VISUAL_SYSTEM`, a theme variant overrides colors only, an unknown family throws) and `themeCssVariables` (`--rc-bg`, `--rc-text`, `--rc-accent`, `--rc-font-display`, `--rc-safe-margin`, …). `brands.visual_system.fonts` names families by id (`fraunces`, `inter`).
- Impact on plan: M3-07 reads these helpers; when B-10 delivers brand fonts, add them to `FONT_FAMILIES` and set `visual_system.fonts`.

## 2026-10-10 · Template framework: string HTML, not React components [M3-07]
- Context: 08 §8.2 sketched a React component per template. The renderer and the preview route need the same page, with fonts and images inlined, and nothing else.
- Decision: templates render to **HTML strings** (`templates/src/framework`, exported from `@rc/templates/render`), no React or react-dom in the package: `renderSlideHtml({ slide, theme, assets, page })` returns one self-contained 1080 × 1350 document (fonts and pictures as data URLs, the CSS variables of the theme on `.rc-slide`, a logo at the template's anchor, a text mark until the logo asset exists (dropped 2026-10-10: no mark without a logo), the page indicator `n/m`, and the fit-text script). Each template is a `TemplateRenderer` `{ css, body(context) }` registered with `registerRenderer` (M3-08 … M3-10); `textSlot` and `imageSlot` build the slot markup from the template metadata, so the writer's limits, the validators and the render share one source: a text slot lives in a fixed box of `maxLines × maxPx × line-height` (1.12 display, 1.3 body), the script (`FIT_TEXT_SCRIPT`, plain ES5, waits for `document.fonts.ready`) lowers the size by 2 px until the text fits or the minimum is reached and then sets `data-overflow="true"` (QA blocker); `data-image-missing` marks an empty image box. All text is HTML-escaped. Hyphenation is off.
- Impact on plan: 08 §8.2's `component` field is replaced by the registered renderer; a browser preview component is a thin wrapper around the same HTML (M3-13 uses an iframe).

## 2026-10-10 · Playwright renderer and render QA [M3-11]
- Decision: `@rc/modules/visuals/render` (a separate entry: `apps/web` must not import it, dependency rule `web-no-visuals-render`): `renderCarousel({ slides: [{ slide, assets }], theme })` stops before any browser starts when the glyph check finds a missing character (`MISSING_GLYPH`); otherwise one Chromium (`playwright-core`; `launchBrowser`: an explicit path, else a local Chrome on a Mac, else Playwright's own Chromium, as in CI and cloud sessions) with a 1080 × 1350 viewport at scale 1, one page per slide built with `renderSlideHtml`, waits for `html[data-fit-done]`, measures the page, takes a PNG and encodes JPEG q90 4:4:4 sRGB (one re-encode at q85 above 8 MB). `qa.ts`: `buildQaReport` (the `QaReport` of 04 §4.4), `judgeQa` → flags `TEXT_OVERFLOW`, `MISSING_GLYPH`, `VISUAL_MISSING` (required picture empty), `RENDER_FAILED` (picture did not load, size not 1080 × 1350, logo outside ± 2 px of its anchor and height, file over 8 MB). A text slot also counts as overflowing when it leaves the safe margin or touches the logo.
- Findings while making the fixtures pass (they led to fixes in the framework): (1) the font-name quotes in the CSS variables ended the `style` attribute, so the font variables and the safe margin were lost; the value is now HTML-escaped; (2) a tight line height (1.12) makes the font's glyph box count as overflow, so slots carry vertical padding (0.1 em display, 0.06 em body) that is part of their maximum height; (3) a padded label slot inside template C overflowed by its own padding, so the pill is a wrapper; (4) slots no longer shrink in flex layouts (`flex: none`); (5) template D's optional picture is smaller (160 px) and sits under the title so that the longest title and five steps fit. Every fixture of every template (regular and maximum length) now renders with no QA finding.
- A maximum-size text slot is only bounded by the height of its box (`maxLines` at the largest font), not by `maxLines` at the smallest one: text may take more lines at a smaller size. CI installs Chromium for the render tests (`playwright-core install`); without a browser they are skipped.
- Impact on plan: M3-12 passes the browser path from the env; M3-14 turns the fixtures into golden PNGs.

## 2026-10-10 · render-carousel job (J8) and V-17 (Playwright extension) [M3-12, V-17]
- V-17 (Playwright part, read from the installed `@trigger.dev/build` 4.x): `playwright({ browsers, headless, version })` from `@trigger.dev/build/extensions/playwright` installs Chromium and its system libraries in the deploy image, detects the Playwright version itself (pinned here to 1.63.0), and sets `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/ms-playwright`; `playwright-core` finds the browser there without a path. Machine presets include `medium-1x`.
- Decision: `renderVariantCarousel` (`@rc/modules/visuals/render`, job `render-carousel` on the `render` queue, `medium-1x`, 10 min). `loadRenderInput` computes the **input hash** (slides: id, template, slot texts, image slots with asset id and storage key; the theme; the template versions) and `requestRender` queues the job with key `render:{variantId}:{inputHash}` (a `delaySeconds` of 10 for text edits, `RENDER_DEBOUNCE_SECONDS`, used by M4 edits), so the same content is one run. J7 queues J8 when its pictures are done; J5 → J7 → J8. The theme comes from the brand's `visual_system` plus the theme variant of the hypothesis the visual brief applied. The job reuses a READY render of the same input (making it current), otherwise renders and stores `renders/{variantId}/{renderId}/{i}.jpg`, `rendered_slides`, the QA report, sets `current_render_id` and the flags it owns (`TEXT_OVERFLOW`, `MISSING_GLYPH`, `RENDER_FAILED`, `VISUAL_MISSING`: set when found, cleared when the new render no longer finds them). A render with QA findings still produces JPEGs (the reviewer sees them) and is READY; a missing glyph gives a FAILED render with the report and no pictures; a crash marks the render FAILED, flags `RENDER_FAILED` and rethrows so the job retries. The handler loads the render code with a dynamic import so the web app, which registers the handlers, does not load Playwright.
- Asset files: fonts and icons are read through `assetPath` (module-relative, then `./templates/assets`, `./assets`, `../templates/assets`; `setAssetsRoot` pins it) because a bundled job runs elsewhere; `jobs/trigger.config.ts` copies `../templates/assets/**` with `additionalFiles` and keeps `playwright-core` and `sharp` external.
- **Not verified here**: the dev run in Trigger.dev (no project credentials in this session): the bundle's asset lookup, the external packages and the Chromium image must be checked with `pnpm jobs:dev` / `trigger deploy` and a real variant. The inline integration test renders with a real Chromium and fakes for the pictures.

## 2026-10-10 · Slide preview route and carousel viewer [M3-13]
- Decision: `GET|POST /api/preview/slide` (`getSlidePreviewHtml` in `modules/src/visuals/preview.ts`): the page of `renderSlideHtml` for a stored slide, with the slot values of an unsaved draft on top (POST body `slots`; only slots the template has, so a draft cannot add markup), the stored pictures inlined; session required (401 otherwise), `no-store`. It is shown by `SlidePreview` in an iframe with `sandbox="allow-scripts"` and `srcdoc` (fetched, then set), scaled by a CSS transform: no `allow-same-origin`, so its script cannot reach the app, and the global `X-Frame-Options: DENY` stays. `getReviewBundle` adds to each variant `render` (`state` NONE, RENDERING, READY, STALE (the content changed since the current render: the input hash differs), FAILED; presigned JPEG URLs in slide order with the 30-minute preview TTL; the QA report; the error) and `visualBrief`. `CarouselViewer` (review screen, English and Russian strings): 4:5 frame with swipe (scroll snap), previous/next, thumbnails, a 1:1 zoom dialog (1080 × 1350), QA badges (text that does not fit, missing characters, wrong size, logo out of place, "checks passed"), and while a render is stale or running the live preview replaces the JPEG with a "Re-rendering…" badge.
- Evidence: `modules/src/visuals/render/render-variant.test.ts` (preview text and draft, states, and, with a browser, the preview page screenshot equals the exported JPEG pixel for pixel but for about 0.007 % of the values, anti-aliasing at glyph edges between two browser contexts).
- Not done here: UI screenshots (the dev server needs the Supabase login or the E2E bypass); check the review screen in dev with a real render when Trigger.dev is connected.

## 2026-10-10 · Visual regression tests [M3-14]
- Decision: `pnpm test:visual [--update]` (`modules/src/visuals/render/visual-cli.ts`) renders the 30 fixtures (6 templates × 5) with the renderer's own page setup (`renderSlidePng`) and compares each PNG with `templates/__golden__/<platform>/<template>-<fixture>.png` using pixelmatch (per-pixel threshold 0.1, a fail above 0.1 % differing pixels; `golden.ts`, unit-tested). Actual and diff images of a failure go to the git-ignored `templates/__golden__/.actual/`. The fixture picture is a small block image, so the PNGs stay small (2.5 MB for 30). Goldens are **per platform** because Chromium on macOS and on Linux anti-alias text differently: the committed ones are `darwin` (made with a local Chrome); `.github/workflows/visual.yml` (changes to `templates/**` or the renderer, nightly, manual) runs on Ubuntu with the Chromium that `playwright-core` pins and, with no `linux` goldens yet, **fails on its first run and uploads the actual images as the `visual-actual` artifact**: commit those as `templates/__golden__/linux/` (reviewing them) to turn the job green. A deliberate CSS change (the colour of the big number in template B) fails the check; the clean tree passes. The goldens were looked at (A and E): fonts, layout and the logo are as designed.
- Impact on plan: the CI job is green only after the linux goldens are committed (one manual step); `CLAUDE.md` lists the new command.

## 2026-10-10 · Photo library [M3-16]
- Decision: PHOTO sources are now accepted (`.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.png`, `.webp`, up to 25 MB, content type checked against the extension). `completeSourceUpload` queues a new job **`import-library-photo`** (J20) for them instead of `ingest-source`, and the AI-processing gate does not apply (no model reads a photo). `importLibraryPhoto` (`modules/src/visuals/library`): auto-orient, sRGB, at most 2160 px on the long side (never enlarged), WebP q90 at `library/{assetId}.webp`, perceptual hash, a READY `visual_assets` row (`LIBRARY_PHOTO`, `source_asset_id`, `original_key` = the uploaded file, rights copied from the source, description and tags from the source's `metadata_json` or the title) and the source READY; a file that is not an image is FAILED (`NOT_AN_IMAGE`); a repeat finds the asset of the source. `updateLibraryPhoto` sets the description, tags (normalized: lower case, unique, at most 12) and REJECTED/READY. `listLibraryCandidates` (what the visual director is offered; newest 60): READY photos whose source is not archived and whose **source's current rights** allow a visual transform (`visuallyTransform = ALLOWED`, not RESTRICTED), so a later rights change counts at once; `generateVisualAssets` repeats that check before it uses a library photo and fails the slot if it no longer holds.
- Not done: AI tags from a vision model (optional in the task) and an upload/management screen for photos (the source upload screen does not offer the PHOTO type yet; photos can be uploaded through the existing source actions). Add both when the photo library has content.
- Evidence / links: `modules/src/visuals/library/library.test.ts`, the director test in `modules/src/content/visual/visual.test.ts` (a rights-clean photo is offered, picked and stored in the brief; after the rights change it is not offered and picking is refused), `sources.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-10 · Visual QA with vision and the contrast check [M3-17]
- Decision: `visual-qa@1` (`prompts/src/visual-qa`): the rendered JPEGs (one image per slide, in order, before the text) and a list of the slides with their texts; the model reports `LEGIBILITY`, `AI_ARTIFACT`, `TEXT_IN_IMAGE`, `BRAND`, `COMPOSITION` or `OTHER` issues of severity MAJOR or MINOR (never BLOCKER: a model that looks at pictures does not stop an approval) and is told not to repeat the app's own checks, to judge neither the wording nor the facts, and that an empty list is a good answer. `runVisualQa` (`modules/src/visuals/visual-qa.ts`, job `run-visual-qa` J21 on the `llm` queue, action `runVisualQa`; **started on demand**, not after every render, so the cost follows the person's request): uses the current READY render, validates that every issue names a slide that was sent (one repair), and replaces the variant's previous `VISUAL_*` issues in `critic_report.issues` (category `VISUAL_<CATEGORY>`, path `slides.<slideId>`), keeping the critic's own. A slide that shows a **library photo whose source does not allow AI processing is not sent** (the model would see the photo); with every slide left out no model is called. Without a critic report the findings are returned but not stored.
- Contrast (08 §8.6, P1): the renderer takes a second screenshot with the text hidden and, for every text slot, computes the 5th-percentile WCAG contrast of the slot's colour over the pixels under its box (`worstContrast`); below 4.5:1 the slot is listed in `qa_report.lowContrast` and in the verdict's `warnings` (no flag, no block). The regular fixtures of all six templates pass; a light-on-light theme is caught.
- Also: the review screen now shows the critic's `hook` score (M2-12a, added to the score list in both languages).
- Not done: the **real run on 5 variants** needs a model call and rendered variants; the logging of that run waits for the first real renders (together with the G1 pilot).

UI, tests, comments and docs use neutral wording; the names of the cloud resources (Supabase, R2, Sentry, Trigger.dev, Meta apps) are unchanged because they name real resources.

On the owner's ratings sunburst won 2 briefs, flare 1, 5 ties, equal mean scores (4.38); the owner decided on those ratings alone (Sergey had not rated). Record: `docs/decisions/image-provider.md`.

Latency: flare 17 s, sunburst 30 s (median).

## 2026-10-11 · Variant state machine [M4-02]
- Decision: `modules/src/content/variants/state.ts` holds the transition table of 10 §10.4.3 as data (`VARIANT_TRANSITIONS`: from, event, to, guards, effects, audit action), pure and database-free. Events are `START_PIPELINE`, `PIPELINE_SUCCESS/FAILURE`, `EDIT` (an edit or a field regeneration), `REQUEST_CHANGES`, `RESUBMIT`, `APPROVE`, `REJECT`, `UNAPPROVE`, `SCHEDULE`, `PUBLISH_NOW`, `CANCEL_SCHEDULE`, `DISPATCH`, `PUBLISH_SUCCESS/FAILURE`, `RETRY` and `RETRY_SCHEDULED` (row 17 has two targets). `allowedActions(facts, role)` is what the UI reads: each action with `enabled`, `blockers` (render missing/running/stale/failed, blocking flag, missing offer, role), `confirm` and, for approve, the `checklist` (`factsChecked`; `safetyChecked` with SAFETY_REVIEW; `manychatConfigured` with a COMMENT_KEYWORD or DM_KEYWORD CTA). `approvalBlockers` and `approvalChecklist` are exported for M4-06.
- `transitionVariant` (`variants/transition.ts`) applies a status-changing row through the shared `transition` helper (one audit event named by the row), bumps `lock_version` and `status_changed_at`, and for rows 13 and 18 cancels the open (SCHEDULED or QUEUED) publications with an audit event each and, for row 18, clears `approved_version_id`. Approval snapshots, campaign ids, review events and publications are written by the services that own those events (M4-03, M4-06, M4-08). Events that keep the status (an edit in review) are not transitions and `transitionVariant` refuses them.
- Not done here: the pipeline (`generate-variants.ts`) still moves its own rows through `transition`; it keeps working unchanged and can be switched later.
- Evidence: `modules/src/content/variants/variants.test.ts` (the full 10 × 17 status × event matrix against a second, hand-written table; allowed actions per status; every blocking flag; checklist; cancel-and-clear effect). Test fixtures: `variants/test-support.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · Field paths and the edit service [M4-03]
- Decision (`modules/src/content/variants/field-path.ts`, `edit.ts`, `content.ts`; action `editVariantField`): `FieldPath` is parsed into `hook | caption | cta | hashtags | visualBrief | slide | slot | image` (slides by their stable id, never by position) with `readField`, `writeField` (a copy), a Zod shape per kind (hook ≤ 300 characters, hashtags ≤ 30, a whole slide keeps its id and position) and `affectsRender`. `editVariantField` takes `{variantId, lockVersion, field, value, reasonCode?, reason?, confirmed?}`:
  - the status must allow `EDIT` in the state machine; a stale `lockVersion` is a CONFLICT whose message names the last reviewer (from the latest review event) and carries the current lock version, and it is checked again under a row lock;
  - the value must pass the checks that belong to its field: template limits and unknown slots/slides (`validateSlots`), caption ≤ 2,200, hashtag format, CTA keyword, links and emoji on slides (`validateTextRules`), market forbidden patterns, citations (an edited slide may cite only the idea's cards). A BLOCKER inside the edited field refuses the edit (`ValidationError`, `fieldErrors.value`); MAJOR and MINOR findings come back as `warnings`. CTA type must be an active `cta_type`, its offer must belong to the variant's market (and becomes `offer_id`), an image must be a READY asset;
  - **a number the cards do not support does not refuse the edit:** it sets `NUMERIC_MISMATCH` (recomputed on every edit, so fixing the text clears it), which blocks approval. The closed-book rule stays, the reviewer can still save work in progress;
  - one `review_events` row (EDIT) with the value before and after, the reason, the reviewer and the lock version the reviewer saw; audit `variant.edited`; an unchanged value records nothing;
  - editing an APPROVED, SCHEDULED or FAILED variant needs `confirmed: true` (else INVALID_STATE with `requiresConfirmation`), then goes through `transitionVariant`: READY_FOR_REVIEW, approval cleared, the open publication cancelled (ids returned), audit `variant.approval_invalidated` and `publication.cancelled`; the approved version row stays as history;
  - slide edits queue the render with the 10 s debounce (picture changes at once); a failure to queue is logged and does not undo the edit (the screen shows the render as stale).
- Also: `selectExemplars` (M2-17) now counts a draft's edits from review events (EDIT and REGENERATE) instead of the `lock_version − 1` proxy; `assertReasonCode` moved to `variants/reason.ts` and is shared with the regeneration requests.
- Not done: voice examples are not yet created from hook edits (M2-17 reads them, the writer of the pairs comes with the review screen follow-ups); regeneration of one field is M4-04.
- Evidence: `variants/edit.test.ts` (18 cases), `variants/field-path.test.ts`, `voice.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · Field regeneration [M4-04]
- Decision: prompt **`field-regenerator@1`** (`prompts/src/field-regenerator`; stage FIELD_REGENERATION, effort high, 8,000 tokens, already in `STAGE_CONFIG`). Input: the usual writer context (voice, idea, cards, market profile and plan, conversion table, offer, slot limits, sibling summary, taxonomy) plus the whole current draft, the field (path and kind), for an image slot the current picture plan, the reviewer's note (≤ 500 characters) and the reason code with its label. Output `{field, value, rationale}`; `fieldRegeneratorOutputFor(input)` makes `field` the given path and types `value` per kind (hook/caption/slot: string; hashtags: string[]; cta: type from the taxonomy; slide: role, template, slots, cited cards of the idea, factual, altText; image: prompt, negativePrompt, composition). It opens with the brand-free `CONTENT_INTRO` from the start (no `independentVersion` needed; `independent.test.ts` checks it).
- Action `regenerateVariantField` (`requestFieldRegeneration`): the field must exist (slide, template slot, or an image slot that has a picture plan; the picture plan itself is `regenerateVisualBrief`), the status must allow `EDIT`, the lock version must be current, the reason code a taxonomy term; an approved variant needs `confirmed` because the result undoes the approval. Audit `variant.field_regeneration_requested`; queues **J6 `regenerate-field`** (`jobs/src/tasks/regenerate-field.ts`, llm queue, 5 min) with key `regen:{requestId}` and returns `{jobRunId, requestId}`.
- Job (`runFieldRegeneration`, in `variants/regenerate.ts`): loads the pipeline context for the one variant, asks the model through `runStage` with the edit checks as validator (template limits, text rules, forbidden patterns, citations, numeric fidelity: here a number the cards do not support IS a blocker, because the model must obey the closed book; one repair), then applies the value through the same `applyFieldChange` as a manual edit with action REGENERATE, the reviewer = the person who asked, `generation_run_id` and the note as the reason. Outcomes, not exceptions: `APPLIED`, `UNCHANGED`, `CONFLICT` (the lock version moved while the model worked: the result is dropped, audit `variant.field_regeneration_discarded`), `SKIPPED` (status changed, unconfirmed approval, no draft), `FAILED` (no usable value). Transient errors are rethrown; a retry after success finds the lock moved and does nothing. Undo is an ordinary edit back (M4-05).
- Image slot: the model writes a new prompt, negative prompt and composition for that slot; the picture plan entry is updated (source GENERATE, library id dropped), the review event holds the old and new plan entry, and J7 `generate-visual-assets` is queued for that one slot (key `regen-image:{requestId}`); J7 queues the render when the picture is there. The new picture is therefore not in the review event.
- Also: `applyFieldChange` (shared by edit and regeneration) is exported from `variants/edit.ts`; `ai.test.ts` now expects an unknown version instead of the missing `field-regenerator@1`.
- Not done: no real model run (cost); run a few regenerations on the dev variants when the review screen (M4-05) exists, and record cost and acceptance there.
- Evidence: `variants/regenerate.test.ts` (15 cases: hook, caption, hashtags, CTA, slot, slide, image slot, conflict, repair, unchanged, skip, approved undone with the post cancelled, request checks, queued payload, action → job runner → applied), `prompts/src/field-regenerator/v1.test.ts` (snapshot, per-kind output schema, hostile note).

## 2026-10-11 · Review screen v1: editing and regeneration per field [M4-05]
- Decision: `/content/review/[ideaId]` edits one field at a time (components in `apps/web/src/components/review/`). **`VariantProvider`** (client) holds what one market's editors share: the lock version every edit sends (taken from the server on each refresh, advanced locally after a save), whether editing is allowed and why not (`allowed` actions of the bundle, M4-02), the confirmation before an approval is undone, the CONFLICT state, the open slide and `focusField`. Fields: **`EditableText`** (hook, caption, slots; ✎ and ↻, counter with the template's `max_chars`, `max_lines` and "required", Save off when unchanged or over the limit; the server's refusal is shown next to the field), **`EditableHashtags`** (separated by spaces), **`EditableCta`** (type from the taxonomy, text, keyword for COMMENT_KEYWORD/DM_KEYWORD), **`SlideCard`** and the **slide drawer** (slots with counters and limits, picture slots with a regenerate button, cited cards, live preview of the saved slide). `useEditField` calls `editVariantField`; `RegeneratePopover` (instruction ≤ 500, reason code) calls `regenerateVariantField` and then polls `getStatuses` (which now returns `lockVersion`) every 2.5 s for up to 2 minutes until the draft changes.
- After a regeneration the field shows **old versus new with Keep and Undo** (`RegenerationDiff`): the bundle returns the fields whose latest review event is a REGENERATE (`VariantView.regenerations`), Undo is an ordinary `editVariantField` back to the old value (recorded), Keep hides the box on this device (localStorage, per review event; the screen works without storage). A picture can only be kept (the old picture stays in storage but is not a field value).
- **Conflict handling:** a CONFLICT keeps the person's text in its open editor, shows a banner with Reload and a toast with Reload; nothing is lost. **Approved drafts:** the edit button asks first ("Send back to review?", with the effect named per status: approved, scheduled, failed) and then sends `confirmed: true`.
- **Critic panel:** every issue and unsupported claim with a field path has a "go to the field" button that scrolls to it, opens its slide drawer and flashes it. `ReviewPoller` also refreshes the page every 3 s while a carousel is rendering or stale (15 minutes at most), so a re-render after an edit shows up.
- The bundle's `VariantView` gained `allowed` (role-aware) and `regenerations`; `getStatuses` gained `lockVersion`. All new texts are in both catalogs (`review.edit.*`).
- **Component tests:** jsdom with `@testing-library/react`, `user-event` and `jest-dom` added to `apps/web` as dev dependencies; the root `vitest.config.ts` (projects per package, unchanged) now compiles JSX with the automatic runtime (`apps/web/tsconfig.json` has `jsx: preserve` for Next) and maps `@/` to `apps/web/src`; `apps/web/vitest.config.ts` does the same for `pnpm --filter @rc/web test` run alone. `review-editing.test.tsx` (13 cases): save with the lock version, cancel, counter and limit, the server's refusal, conflict with the text kept and Reload, a blocked editor, confirmation accepted and declined, regenerate with instruction and reason and the wait for the change, Undo, Keep (and remembered), picture-only Keep, jump to a field.
- Not done here: the approve, reject, request-changes and un-approve buttons and dialogs (M4-06); "Schedule…" (M4-09); keyboard shortcuts (P1). The task's "manual walkthrough in dev; screenshots" needs a signed-in session on the dev database; it is done together with the E2E spec of M4-10, which drives this screen with the scripted model and takes the screenshots.
- Evidence: `apps/web/src/components/review/review-editing.test.tsx`, `modules/src/content/variants/review-view.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · Approve, reject, request changes, un-approve [M4-06]
- Decision (`modules/src/content/variants/approve.ts`, `decisions.ts`, `render-state.ts`; web actions `approveVariant`, `requestChanges`, `resubmitVariant`, `rejectVariant`, `unapproveVariant`, `triggerRender`):
  - **`approveVariant`** `{variantId, lockVersion, checklist}`: READY_FOR_REVIEW only; stale lock → CONFLICT; guards from the state machine's `approvalBlockers` (current render READY and **made from the current content**, i.e. its input hash equals `loadRenderInput` now; no blocking flag; a commercial idea has an offer) → INVALID_STATE with `details.blockers`; the checklist (`factsChecked` always, `safetyChecked` with SAFETY_REVIEW, `manychatConfigured` with a COMMENT_KEYWORD/DM_KEYWORD CTA) → VALIDATION with `checklist.<item>` field errors; a last run of the blocking validators over the whole draft, so a finding that slipped past the flags (an edit around the service) still stops it. Then one transaction, under a row lock: next `version_no`, a `content_variant_versions` row with the `ApprovedSnapshot` (hook, caption, CTA, hashtags, slides, template sequence, offer, render id, **the storage keys of the render's images in order**, cited cards with their versions, generation version), `content_hash` = SHA-256 of the snapshot in canonical form (`hashSnapshot`), `approved_by`; the variant goes APPROVED with `approved_version_id` through `transitionVariant`; review event APPROVE; audit `variant.approved`. Only a person can approve (a SYSTEM or JOB actor is refused). Withdrawing an approval and approving again gives version 2; version 1 stays.
  - **"Snapshot equals render hash":** the guard compares the render's input hash with the content's at approval time, the version stores that render's id and image keys, and `verifyApprovedVersion(versionId)` re-checks later that the stored hash matches the snapshot, the render exists and is READY, its images are exactly the snapshot's keys, and the slide count agrees. Campaign id and UTM join the snapshot in M4-11.
  - **`requestChanges`** (reason code from the taxonomy + what should change, required), **`resubmitVariant`**, **`rejectVariant`** (reason code required, details optional; final; the (idea, market) slot is free again), **`unapproveVariant`** (reason required; APPROVED, SCHEDULED or FAILED → READY_FOR_REVIEW, open publication cancelled, `approved_version_id` cleared). Each writes a review event (reviewer, reason code, reason, the lock version it found) and an audit event through the state machine.
  - **`triggerRender`** (listed in 05 §5.7, no task owned it) queues the render of the draft as it is; the review screen offers "Render again" when a stale, missing or failed render is what blocks approval.
- UI (`variant-actions.tsx`): buttons driven by `variant.allowed`; Approve is disabled while blocked and the blockers are listed below (translated by code); the approval dialog shows only the checklist items that apply and sends the lock version; request changes, reject and withdraw approval are reason dialogs (reject and withdraw name their effect); a CONFLICT also raises the banner. "Schedule…" arrives with M4-09.
- Evidence: `variants/approve.test.ts` (9 cases: the snapshot and its binding to the render, tampering found by `verifyApprovedVersion`, every render state, every blocking flag, commercial without/with offer, the three checklist boxes, stale lock, wrong statuses, a draft that fails the validators without a flag, version 2 after withdrawing, only a person), `variants/decisions.test.ts` (6), `apps/web/src/components/review/variant-actions.test.tsx` (11).

## 2026-10-11 · Draft queue, dashboard counters and nav badges [M4-07]
- Decision (`modules/src/content/variants/queries.ts`, `/content/drafts`, `/dashboard`, sidebar): **`listDrafts`** returns the queue for a tab, newest change first, 25 per page: idea (links to its review screen), market with flag, status, quality score, flags, last update, and **who touched it last** (the latest review event with a reviewer; none until M4's edits exist for a draft). Tabs group statuses: In review (DRAFT, GENERATING, READY_FOR_REVIEW, CHANGES_REQUESTED), Approved, Scheduled (SCHEDULED, PUBLISHING), Published, Failed, Rejected; each tab shows its count, and the **market filter** and the **"flags only" filter** apply to the counts too. Filters, tab and page live in the address (`lib/drafts-query.ts`, same pattern as ideas and cards); a page past the end redirects to the last one. REJECTED drafts are in their own tab, not hidden.
- **`getWorkQueue`** gives the counters in one query (the layout runs it on every page, and a database round trip is the cost that counts): cards to review, ideas proposed, drafts ready for review, changes requested, approved not scheduled, publications going out in the next 7 days (SCHEDULED or QUEUED, dry runs excluded, late ones included) and failed variants. `/dashboard` shows them as counters that link to where they are worked off (the calendar links work from M4-09; failed opens the Failed tab); health tiles and recent activity stay with H-01 and the page says so.
- **Sidebar badges** (`NavItem.badge`, only above zero, 99+ capped): Ideas = proposed, Knowledge Base = cards to review, Drafts = ready for review + changes requested, Calendar = approved not scheduled.
- All texts in both catalogs (`drafts.*`, `dashboard.*`).
- Evidence: `variants/queries.test.ts` (5 cases: tabs, order and counts; market and flag filters; paging; the last person; every counter with cancelled and dry-run publications left out), `lib/drafts-query.test.ts`, `screens.test.ts` (badges).

## 2026-10-11 · Scheduling service [M4-08]
- Decision (`modules/src/publishing/schedule.ts`, `time.ts`; web actions `schedulePublication`, `reschedulePublication`, `cancelPublication` for owner and editor):
  - **`schedulePublication`** `{variantId, mode, scheduledAt (ISO, UTC), dryRun}`: only an APPROVED variant with its approved version (a SCHEDULED one is told to change its time instead); the time must be at least **5 minutes ahead** of the context's clock (VALIDATION on `scheduledAt`, exactly five is accepted); the approved version must still match its render (`verifyApprovedVersion`); then one transaction inserts the SCHEDULED publication (explicit id, **idempotency key `pub:{id}:0`**, `social_account_id` empty until dispatch, `created_by`), moves the variant to SCHEDULED through the state machine and writes `publication.scheduled`. A second live post for the same draft is refused by the status, and under a race by the partial unique index (the driver's wrapped error is recognised and becomes a CONFLICT). **`IMMEDIATE` and `dryRun` are refused with a clear message until M5**: they need the dispatcher, the account and the kill switch, and a variant moved to PUBLISHING with nothing to publish it would be stuck.
  - **Warnings, never errors** (returned with the result): `NO_ACTIVE_ACCOUNT` (the market has no ACTIVE `social_accounts` row), `PUBLISHING_DISABLED` (`publishing.enabled` is off, the seeded default), `MIN_GAP` (another live, non-dry-run post of the same market, scheduled or published, within `publishing.min_gap_minutes`, default 180, 0 switches it off; it names the nearest post). `scheduleWarnings` is exported for the calendar dialog.
  - **`reschedulePublication`**: SCHEDULED only (a QUEUED one is already with the dispatcher), the same 5-minute rule, the same warnings (ignoring the post itself), the idempotency key unchanged (only a retry makes a new one), audit `publication.rescheduled` with the old and new time.
  - **`cancelPublication`** `{publicationId, reason}`: SCHEDULED or QUEUED; the publication becomes CANCELLED with `cancelled_by` and `cancelled_at`, the variant returns to APPROVED (`CANCEL_SCHEDULE`) and can be scheduled again as a new publication of the same version; the reason is in the audit event.
  - **Market time zone:** the services take and store UTC (05 §5.1). `publishing/time.ts` (also exported as `@rc/modules/publishing/time`, pure, for the calendar's client code) converts the market's wall clock to UTC: `zonedToUtc` moves a time that does not exist (clocks going forward) to the hour after and says `adjusted`, takes the first of a time that happens twice (clocks going back); `formatLocalDateTime`, `localParts`, `localDate`, `offsetLabel` show a UTC instant in a zone. `describeSlot` words a confirmation ("2026-10-20 13:00 (UTC+2)").
- Evidence: `publishing/schedule.test.ts` (16 cases: the publication and variant, every time rule, immediate and dry run refused, one live post and the race, statuses, a damaged version, the three warnings and the settings that switch them off, move, cancel and schedule again, queued and finished posts), `publishing/time.test.ts` (offsets, parsing, both DST edges, round trips in Madrid, New York and Sydney).

## 2026-10-11 · Calendar UI [M4-09]
- Decision (`modules/src/publishing/calendar.ts`, `/content/calendar`, `components/calendar/`, `lib/calendar-query.ts`):
  - **`getCalendar({from, to, marketIds?})`** (05 §5.8): the non-dry-run publications SCHEDULED, QUEUED, IN_PROGRESS, PUBLISHED or FAILED in a half-open range, earliest first, each with its market (code, name, flag, **time zone**, sort order for the color), the approved version's hook as the title, a presigned thumbnail of the version's first image, the permalink, and warnings: `NO_ACTIVE_ACCOUNT`, `PUBLISHING_DISABLED` and `OVERDUE` (a post that still waits after its time) for waiting posts, `MIN_GAP` for any post with a neighbour of the same market closer than `publishing.min_gap_minutes`. **`listApprovedNotScheduled`** gives the side list (APPROVED variants, longest waiting first, with thumbnail).
  - **Week grid** (default) of seven day columns, today marked, each post a card with time, market flag, thumbnail, title, status badge and a warning icon; a **list view** of the same week as a table; previous/next week and "This week". Both views and the week live in the address. Times are drawn in **one zone, chosen with the zone links** (default: the first active market's); the dialogs always speak the post's own market zone. A week with a clock change is 167 or 169 hours long and is cut at local midnights (`weekRange`).
  - **Dialogs** (`ScheduleButton` in the side list, `PostButton` on every post): the time is typed as the market's wall clock in a `datetime-local` input and sent as UTC (`zonedToUtc`); a time inside the skipped hour is moved and the dialog says so; the person's own time is shown as a hint; the button stays off for a time under five minutes away; the server's warnings are shown in the dialog after scheduling. A post offers "Open in review", the Instagram link when published, **Move** (SCHEDULED only) and **Call off** (SCHEDULED or QUEUED, with a reason). Chefs see the dialog read-only (the actions are for owners and editors).
  - `MarketSummary` gained `timezone`; `@rc/modules/publishing/time` is exported for the client code. Texts in both catalogs (`calendar.*`).
- Not done: dragging a post to another day (P1), a month view; screenshots with the E2E spec (M4-10).
- Evidence: `publishing/calendar.test.ts` (4: order, range edges, market and status filters, all warnings including a late post, the side list and cancel returning a draft to it), `lib/calendar-query.test.ts` (8, including the 167-hour week), `components/calendar/calendar-dialogs.test.tsx` (11: Madrid wall clock → UTC, the skipped hour, warnings after scheduling, the server's refusal, move, call off with a reason, read-only roles, published and queued posts).

## 2026-10-11 · Review → schedule E2E, spec written (not yet run green) [M4-10]
- Decision: `apps/web/e2e/review.e2e.ts` (an editor): generate an idea → accept → drafts for both markets → the draft queue and the dashboard counter show them → **edit the hook** → **regenerate slide 2** with a note (old vs new shown, Keep) → wait for the new render → **approve** with the checklist → open the calendar, **schedule** the draft from the "approved, not scheduled" list (the dialog reports that publishing is off and no account is connected) → the post is on its day of the week grid → **move** it → **call it off** (the draft is back in the side list). It takes screenshots (`review-edited.png`, `calendar.png`) into Playwright's output directory, not into git.
- Supporting changes: the scripted E2E model answers `field-regenerator` (the current value plus "(redone)", per kind; `e2e-llm.test.ts` checks every answer against the prompt's own output schema); `removeIdeasOfSource` now also removes the posts, review events, approved versions, renders and pictures of the drafts it deletes (they reference the variants).
- **Status: green.** Migration `0006` was applied to the dev database (`pnpm db:migrate`, 2026-10-11) and the whole suite passes against the production build: `drafts`, `ideas`, `knowledge`, `offers` and the new `review` spec (about 40 s). CI still does not run the E2E specs (they need a Supabase project, B-02).
- **What the first runs found:**
  - **Renders and picture jobs bumped `lock_version`**, so a reviewer's lock went stale just because a render finished: an edit or a field regeneration then ended in CONFLICT (the regeneration silently, as a dropped result). The lock guards people's edits, not the system's: `renderVariantCarousel` and `generateVisualAssets` no longer bump it. Both also wrote from a copy read before a long run (the render's flags, the asset job's whole `slides_json`), which could undo a reviewer's edit made meanwhile; they now merge into the row's latest state (the asset job under a row lock, placing only its pictures into the latest slides; the render keeps the row's current flags and replaces only those it owns). `applyFieldChange` likewise builds its columns from the locked row. Regression test in `generate-assets.test.ts`.
  - Three expectations of the older specs were stale and are corrected: the quality score of the scripted review is 4.38 since the hook score was added (M2-12a); an idea's reject button stays off until a reason is typed; the idea the scripted model builds on depends on the order the database returns the two seeded cards in.
  - The cleanup after a test could be refused when the server's inline jobs were still writing rows for the drafts; it retries a few times. Rows left by the first failing runs were removed by hand.
  - On a 1280 px screen the seven day columns of the calendar were too narrow; the week grid is two or four columns and seven only from 1700 px.
- Note: `playwright test` does not return on its own here (it waits on the server it started), the tests themselves finish; stop the server on port 3100 after a run.

## 2026-10-11 · Campaign IDs, UTM links and the ManyChat panel [M4-11]
- Decision (`modules/src/offers/campaigns.ts`, `utm.ts`; approval in `content/variants/approve.ts`; `CampaignPanel` on the review screen):
  - **Campaign id** `rc-{market}-{yymmdd}-{6 chars}` (e.g. `rc-es-261011-k3f9qa`): the language part of the market code in lower case, the UTC date of the approval, six random characters of `a–z0–9` from `crypto.randomInt`. `planCampaign` keeps the id a variant already has (an approval that is withdrawn and given again keeps its links valid) or draws a new one and checks that no variant has it (up to 8 draws; the column is also unique). It runs for the variants of an idea with a commercial intent other than NONE, which is exactly when approval demands an offer; a post that sells nothing has no campaign.
  - **UTM** (`buildUtm`, `buildUtmUrl`): `utm_json` = source `instagram`, medium `social`, campaign = the campaign id, content = the first eight characters of the variant's id; `url` = the **offer's landing URL** with `utm_source`, `utm_medium`, `utm_campaign`, `utm_content` (existing `utm_*` tags are replaced, other parameters and the fragment kept, values encoded; anything that is not http(s) gives no URL). An offer without a landing page gives the tags only and the panel says so.
  - **At approval** the id and `utm_json` are written with the transition, they are in the **approved snapshot** (`campaignId`, `utm`, `offerId`), in the audit event, and `verifyApprovedVersion` still holds. The ManyChat checklist item for keyword CTAs was already part of the approval (M4-06).
  - **Review screen:** `VariantView` has `commercial` and `campaign {campaignId, keyword, url}`; the panel (commercial posts only) shows the CTA keyword, the DM link with its UTM tags and the **flow name (= campaign id)**, each with a copy button (toast on success, a hint when the clipboard is refused); before approval it says the id and link are made then. Texts in both catalogs (`review.campaign.*`).
- Not done: a one-click "ManyChat flow set up" record beyond the approval checklist (manual in the MVP, [S§9.3]); attribution events and the commercial dashboard are M6-08.
- Evidence: `offers/campaigns.test.ts` (id shape, market and date, injected randomness, no repeats in 2,000 draws, UTM encoding and replacement, bad URLs), `content/variants/campaign-plan.test.ts` (`planCampaign` for a new, an existing and a taken id), `content/variants/approve.test.ts` (campaign in the row, snapshot and audit; same id after re-approval; two posts differ; a non-commercial post has none), `review-view.test.ts`, `campaign-panel.test.tsx` (copying each field, what is missing, before approval, clipboard refused).

## 2026-10-11 · Meta verification pass [M5-00, V-01 … V-16]
- Context: M5-00 checks every ⚠ Meta item against the official docs before M5 coding. Read on 2026-10-11 from `developers.facebook.com` (Instagram Platform overview, Content Publishing, Business Login, the media container and insights references, Graph API error handling and versions, the data deletion callback, the Instagram Platform changelog). The pages were read through a summarizing fetch tool, so the wording below is a summary, and the items marked **smoke** still need the real accounts (M5-10 / G2).
- Results:
  - **V-01 ✓** Standard Access is the default level and is meant for "your Instagram professional account or an account you manage"; Advanced Access (App Review + Business Verification) is for accounts you do not own or manage. Publishing and insights work with Standard Access for both login types. Our accounts are owned, so no App Review. The docs do not say what roles an app needs in Development mode: keep adding the account owners as testers (B-11). **Open:** an account must be a **professional account**; one older page also says "connected to a Page" for the Facebook-login path only, the Instagram-login path needs no Page (smoke).
  - **V-02 ✓** Current scopes: `instagram_business_basic`, `instagram_business_content_publish`, `instagram_business_manage_comments`, `instagram_business_manage_messages`, and the insights reference names `instagram_business_manage_insights` for Instagram Login. The Business Login page does not list the insights scope in its short list; the insights reference does. We request `basic`, `content_publish`, `manage_insights` (insights needed from M6; asking now avoids a second consent). If the authorize page rejects the insights scope, drop it from `INSTAGRAM_SCOPES` and re-check at M6-02. Old scope names were retired on 2025-01-27.
  - **V-03 ✓** Authorize `https://www.instagram.com/oauth/authorize` (`client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `response_type=code`, `scope`, `state`; a newer optional `enable_fb_login`, default true, 2026-02-06). Code → short-lived token: `POST https://api.instagram.com/oauth/access_token` (`client_id`, `client_secret`, `grant_type=authorization_code`, `redirect_uri`, `code`); the answer is `{ data: [{ access_token, user_id, permissions }] }` (the adapter also accepts the older flat shape). Long-lived: `GET https://graph.instagram.com/access_token?grant_type=ig_exchange_token&client_secret&access_token`, lifetime 60 days (5,183,944 s). Refresh: `GET https://graph.instagram.com/refresh_access_token?grant_type=ig_refresh_token&access_token`; the token must be **at least 24 h old**, valid, and carry `instagram_business_basic`.
  - **V-04 ✓ (partly)** Host `graph.instagram.com`. Latest Graph API version **v26.0** (2026-07-29, expiry TBD); v25.0 (2026-02-18 → 2028-07-29), v24.0 (2025-10-08 → 2028-02-18). The docs' own samples use **v25.0**. We pin **v25.0** in `META_GRAPH_API_VERSION` (the one the samples use); upgrading is a deliberate smoke-tested change. **Authorization header:** the content-publishing page's samples show `Authorization: Bearer <ACCESS_TOKEN>`, the `/me` page shows `access_token` in the query. The adapter sends the **Bearer header** for Graph calls (no token in any URL, so it can never reach a log or a trace), and falls back to the query parameter if a call answers with an OAuth error to the header (smoke). The OAuth exchange endpoints take the secret/token as form or query parameters by design.
  - **V-05 ✓** JPEG only (MPO/JPS not supported); aspect ratio within 4:5 to 1.91:1; width 320–1440 px (scaled into range); file ≤ 8 MB; carousel **up to 10** items (the docs give no minimum; we keep 2 as the product rule); carousel images are cropped to the first item's ratio (default 1:1) — our slides are all 1080 × 1350; caption ≤ 2,200 characters, ≤ 30 hashtags, ≤ 20 @ tags; `alt_text` ≤ 1,000 characters. The media URL must be on a publicly reachable server at the time of the attempt (Meta "cURLs" it).
  - **V-06 ✓** **100** API-published posts per rolling 24 h per account, a carousel counts as one (the "50" figure is not in the current docs). The response fields of `content_publishing_limit` are not documented on the pages read; the adapter reads `data[0].quota_usage` and `data[0].config.quota_total` defensively and treats a missing value as unknown, never as exhausted (smoke).
  - **V-07 ✓** `status_code`: `EXPIRED` (not published within 24 h), `ERROR`, `FINISHED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `PUBLISHED`. Polling advice: once per minute, no more than 5 minutes. The plan said 30–60 s up to 10 minutes; we poll every 30 s for the first 3 minutes, then every 60 s, **up to 10 minutes** (a carousel of ten images is slower than a single image), and the wait is a Trigger.dev `wait.for`, so it costs no compute.
  - **V-08 ? open** The docs say nothing about `media_publish` on an already published container. The design does not rely on it: before every publish the container status is read, `PUBLISHED` goes to reconciliation. Test in the smoke run (M5-10) with a throw-away container and record the result in the runbook.
  - **V-09 ◐** Error object: `message`, `code`, `error_subcode`, `error_user_title`, `error_user_msg`, `fbtrace_id`. Documented codes: 1 and 2 temporary (retry), 4 and 17 throttling, 341 application limit, 368 policy block (wait, retry), 10 permission denied, 200–299 permission, 190 token (subcodes 458 not installed, 459 checkpointed, 460 password changed, 463 expired, 467 invalid). **Codes 32, 100 and 613 are not in the page** (they stay in the table from memory and are marked "unverified" in code); no table of subcodes for media errors exists, so the INVALID_MEDIA class is decided by `code 100` + the container going to `ERROR`, plus a few message keywords. The table in `instagram/errors.ts` adds 341 (RATE_LIMIT) and 368 (RATE_LIMIT, longer wait) and the 190 subcodes (459/460 → AUTH with a "log in to Instagram" hint). Unknown codes are collected from `publish_attempts` in the smoke run.
  - **V-10 ✓ (for M6-02)** Feed/carousel media metrics: `comments`, `likes`, `reach`, `profile_visits`, `profile_activity`, `follows`, `reposts`, `saved`, `shares`, `total_interactions`, `views`-family (`total_views`, `facebook_views`); `impressions` is deprecated for media created after 2024-07-02. Periods: day/week/days_28/month/lifetime. Scope for Instagram Login: `instagram_business_basic` + `instagram_business_manage_insights`.
  - **V-11 ◐ (for M6-02)** The account insights reference could not be read (404 on the URL tried); M6-02 re-checks it.
  - **V-12 ✓** Business Use Case rate limit: calls per 24 h = **4,800 × impressions** of the account in the last 24 h. Our volume (< 200 calls a day) fits unless an account has almost no impressions; a new test account may hit it — then the error is RATE_LIMIT and the retry waits.
  - **V-13 ✓** Data deletion callback: `POST`, form field `signed_request` = `base64url(signature).base64url(payload)`; algorithm `HMAC-SHA256` over the **encoded payload** with the app secret; payload fields `algorithm`, `expires`, `issued_at`, `user_id`; the answer is JSON `{ url, confirmation_code }` (status page URL and an alphanumeric code). The deauthorize callback uses the same `signed_request` format (the page read describes only deletion); `parseSignedRequest` from M0-07 already does this. The `user_id` is the **app-scoped** id, so the callbacks match `social_accounts.app_scoped_user_id` and, as a fallback, `instagram_account_id`.
  - **V-14 ? open (smoke)** The docs require a publicly reachable URL and say nothing about query strings or redirects; whether Meta fetches a presigned R2 URL is decided by the smoke run (M5-10). The fallback in plan 09 §9.3 stays.
  - **V-15 ✓ (assumed)** No delete endpoint for media was found in the references read; test posts are deleted by hand.
  - **V-16 ✓ — plan change.** Since **2026-06-22** the Content Publishing API takes **`is_ai_generated=true`** when the media container is created, which applies Instagram's "AI info" label; for a carousel it goes on the **carousel container only**; the field is also readable on the media (`GET /{media-id}?fields=is_ai_generated`). The label cannot be added after publishing. So M5-12's "platform label" option exists: `ai_imagery_disclosure` gets a fourth value `PLATFORM_LABEL`, and the publish machine sets the flag on the parent container when the setting says so and the variant has a generated photo-realistic image. Whether the label is legally enough (EU AI Act transparency), or whether a caption note is needed as well, is B-13.
- Changes to the plan:
  - Migration numbering: M4-01 took `0006` (review/scheduling), so the Instagram operations tables are **`0007_instagram_ops`** (M5-01). Later migrations shift by one (M6 analytics = 0008).
  - 09 §9.2/§9.4/§9.7 updated with the verified values (Bearer header, v25.0, polling, 341/368, AI label). 16 §16.4: V-01 … V-07, V-12, V-13, V-15, V-16 marked verified; V-08, V-14 open until the smoke run; V-09 partly; V-10/V-11 for M6-02.
  - `META_GRAPH_API_VERSION` example becomes `v25.0`.
- Not verifiable here: V-08, V-14 and the exact `content_publishing_limit` shape need a real account (M5-10, B-11).

## 2026-10-11 · Instagram module foundation and migration 0007 [M5-01]
- Decision:
  - **Migration `0007_instagram_ops`** (plan 04 called it 0006; M4-01 took that number): `publish_attempts` (bigserial id, `endpoint` = path only, redacted `request`/`response`, `outcome` OK | TRANSIENT_ERROR | PERMANENT_ERROR | AMBIGUOUS by a check) and `integration_events` (severity check, partial index on unresolved rows). RLS on both, no policies.
  - **`ServiceContext.instagram`** (`InstagramRuntime`: publish mode, the Meta app, the token key ring, the OAuth state secret, and a `fetch` hook for tests/E2E). Built by `instagramRuntimeFromEnv(env)` in the three composition roots (web request and inline jobs, jobs worker, CLI); the default is "off, nothing configured". Services never read env.
  - **`instagram/http.ts`**: `createGraphClient(rt, { accessToken, logger, onCall })` → `get`/`post` against `{graphBaseUrl}/{version}`; the token goes in `Authorization: Bearer` only (V-04); GET parameters in the query, POST parameters as JSON; 30 s timeout via `AbortSignal`; the logger gets the method, the **path without the query**, status, duration and the error class; `onCall` receives a redacted record per call (for `publish_attempts`) before the result is returned or the error thrown. `oauthCall` does the code exchange / long-lived exchange / refresh (outside the versioned API; Meta's `{error_type, code, error_message}` shape is normalized).
  - **`instagram/errors.ts`**: `classifyInstagramError` implements the 09 §9.4.4 table with the M5-00 additions (341, 368, the 190 subcodes, "media not ready" 2207027 → TRANSIENT); `InstagramCallError` carries the stored `InstagramError`, `ambiguous` (a **POST that got no answer** may have taken effect), `retryAfterMs` (60 s default, 5 min for 4/17/613, 15 min for 368) and `toAppError()` (RATE_LIMIT → `TransientError{rateLimited}`, TRANSIENT/UNKNOWN → `TransientError`, the rest → `PermanentError`); `describeInstagramError` gives the readable title and next step the drawer shows (M5-09).
  - **Mock Graph API instead of MSW** (`instagram/mock/graph-mock.ts`, `serve.ts`): one stateful, framework-free `handle(Request) → Response` that models OAuth (code → short → long-lived, refresh after 24 h), `/me`, child and carousel containers, status polling, `media_publish` (a second publish of a container is refused — an assumption, V-08), recent media, `content_publishing_limit`, and `is_ai_generated`. Faults are injected per path: an HTTP error with Meta's body, a connection dropped **before** the call takes effect, or **after** it (the "crash after `media_publish`" case). Unit tests pass `mock.fetch` as `InstagramRuntime.fetch`; `serveMockGraphApi` serves the same handler over HTTP for the E2E server (`INSTAGRAM_GRAPH_BASE_URL`). No new dependency (`msw` would only have wrapped the same fixtures); MSW stays for the vendor-SDK tests in `@rc/lib`.
  - `META_GRAPH_API_VERSION=v25.0` in `.env.example`.
- Not done: `RENDERS_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` (only if V-14 fails in the smoke run); a Graph call counter for the 4,800 × impressions limit (the error class is enough at our volume).
- Evidence: `instagram/instagram.test.ts` (38: the whole classification table, subcodes, readable errors, error → job error mapping, the client's Bearer header, JSON body, **no token or signature in any log line or stored record**, path-only logging, classified errors, ambiguous POST vs GET, timeout, "off" refusal, and the mock's OAuth/publish/refresh flows), `db/src/schema/review.test.ts` (RLS and checks of the new tables).

## 2026-10-11 · OAuth connect flow [M5-02]
- Decision (`modules/src/instagram/{accounts,oauth}.ts`, routes `/api/instagram/oauth/start` and `/callback`, `server/instagram-oauth.ts`):
  - **State**: `signToken({marketId, userId, nonce}, OAUTH_STATE_SECRET)` valid 10 minutes, also kept in an **httpOnly, SameSite=Lax cookie** (`rc-ig-oauth`, path `/api/instagram/oauth`, `secure` in production). The callback needs the state in the URL **and** the cookie to be identical, the HMAC and expiry to hold, and the state's user to be the signed-in owner (a state started by one person cannot be finished by another). Both routes need a session (the proxy) and the owner role.
  - **Authorize URL** asks for the three scopes (`INSTAGRAM_SCOPES`, V-02) with the redirect URI of the environment. In E2E, where `INSTAGRAM_GRAPH_BASE_URL` points to the mock, the authorize and code-exchange hosts follow it.
  - **Callback** (`completeInstagramOAuth`): code (a trailing `#_` is dropped) → short-lived token (accepts both the `{data:[…]}` and the flat answer) → long-lived token (expiry from `expires_in`, else 5,183,944 s) → `/me?fields=user_id,username,account_type` → account type must be BUSINESS or MEDIA_CREATOR (`Business`/`Media_Creator` in the docs: compared upper-case) → the market binding → the encrypted token. Expected failures return `{ ok: false, reason }`, are audited as `instagram.connect_failed` (reason only, never the code), and redirect to `/settings/instagram?error=<reason>`; configuration errors still throw.
  - **Market binding**: an account that is ACTIVE or NEEDS_REAUTH for another market is refused (`bound_to_other_market`); a market that already has a different ACTIVE account is refused (`market_has_other_account`: disconnect first, M5-03); the same account on the same market is a **reconnect in place** (same row, new token, status ACTIVE, open `integration_events` of the account resolved); a REVOKED or DISABLED account may be connected to another market (the table's unique key is `(platform, instagram_account_id)`, so the row is reused).
  - **Token at rest**: `enc:v1:` AES-256-GCM, **AAD = `social_accounts.id`** (the id is generated before the insert), so a token copied to another row does not decrypt. `toAccountView` removes the column for anything that goes to the UI. `appScopedUserId` = `/me.id` (else the code exchange's `user_id`), for the callbacks of M5-05.
  - Two bugs the first run found: the failure audit inside the transaction used the outer connection and deadlocked (PGlite has one connection): the audit now uses the transaction context; `reviewFixture` accepts an `instagram` runtime.
- Evidence: `instagram/oauth.test.ts` (15: URL and scopes; owners only; happy path with the token encrypted, AAD-bound and absent from the audit trail; `#_`; reconnect; tampered, expired, mismatched and foreign state; denied; bad code; non-professional; bound to another market; second account; revoked account moves; profile failure).
- Not done here: the Settings UI that starts and shows this (M5-03); disconnect (M5-03).
