## 2026-10-11 · Token refresh and account health jobs (J13, J14) [M5-04]
- Decision (`instagram/{tokens,health,events}.ts`, job handlers `refresh-instagram-tokens` and `check-account-health`, `jobs/src/tasks/*`, `cronHandlerTask`):
  - **Refresh rule** (`refreshDecision`): never for a token under 24 h old (V-03); otherwise when it expires within 20 days **or** was last refreshed 7+ days ago; else "not due". ACTIVE accounts only. The refresh re-encrypts (AAD = row id), sets the new expiry from `expires_in`, `token_last_refreshed_at`, token status VALID, resolves the open `TOKEN_REFRESH_FAILED` finding, audits `instagram.token_refreshed`.
  - **Failures per account, never for the batch**: code 190 → status NEEDS_REAUTH, token INVALID, ERROR finding `TOKEN_INVALID`, audit `instagram.needs_reauth`; a token whose expiry already passed gets the same without a call; any other failure → WARN finding `TOKEN_REFRESH_FAILED` (ERROR and token status EXPIRING when under 7 days are left). When any account failed for a transient reason the job **throws a `TransientError` after all accounts were handled**, so Trigger.dev retries; the retry touches only accounts that are still due (the 24 h rule makes it idempotent).
  - **Health** (every 6 h at :23): `/me` and `content_publishing_limit` per ACTIVE account; the snapshot `{ok, quotaUsage, quotaTotal, error}` and the time go to `social_accounts.last_health(_check_at)`; a changed user name is followed; an account that is no longer BUSINESS/MEDIA_CREATOR is an ERROR finding `NOT_PROFESSIONAL`. A 190 makes the account NEEDS_REAUTH (same helper as the refresh). Other failures are recorded (one open WARN `HEALTH_CHECK_FAILED`, not repeated) and **do not throw**: the next run tries again. A passing check resolves the findings. NEEDS_REAUTH accounts are not checked.
  - **`integration_events` helpers**: `recordIntegrationEvent` (does not repeat an open finding of the same code for the same account), `resolveIntegrationEvents`.
  - **Cron in code**: `cronHandlerTask(name, cron, options)` is a `schedules.task` (UTC) with the handler's name as task id; it also accepts our envelope when triggered by hand. A scheduled run has no envelope of ours, so the handler gets `{}` (every payload field is optional). Queue `instagram-read`, 10 minutes. 03:13 daily and `*/6 :23` as in 06 §6.4. The retry count comes from `trigger.config.ts` (3, plan 06 §6.2).
  - The mock Graph API now applies an injected **HTTP error before** the call's effect (only `dropAfterEffect` runs it first); it can also issue a token at a given time (its 24 h refresh rule).
- Evidence: `instagram/tokens.test.ts` (23: the decision table, refresh with the old token invalidated and the new one in the ciphertext, the 24 h and not-due skips, 190, already expired, per-account isolation with the retry, EXPIRING, targeting and status filters, health snapshot and calls with Bearer, rename, 190, repeated failure → one finding then resolved, not professional, the jobs with the cron payload), `jobs/src/jobs.test.ts`.
- Not run: a deployed cron on Trigger.dev (needs `trigger deploy` and the Meta app, B-02/B-11); the schedule is declared in code.

## 2026-10-11 · Meta callbacks: deauthorize and data deletion [M5-05, V-13]
- Decision (`instagram/callbacks.ts`, `/api/meta/deauthorize`, `/api/meta/data-deletion`, `/data-deletion`, `server/meta-callbacks.ts`):
  - Both routes are POSTs with a form field `signed_request` (a JSON body with the same field is accepted too). They are public (`/api/meta/` already was in the proxy's list; `/data-deletion` was added): **the HMAC-SHA256 of the encoded payload with the app secret is the only authentication**, checked with M0-07's `parseSignedRequest`. A bad signature, another algorithm, a missing user id or garbage → `InvalidSignedRequestError` → HTTP 400 with a fixed body (nothing from the request is echoed or logged but the reason); publishing `off` (no app secret) → 503.
  - **Deauthorize**: matches accounts by the app-scoped `user_id` **or** the professional account id (we are not sure which one Meta sends for Instagram Login; the docs call it the app-scoped user id); each match becomes REVOKED, its token is destroyed, an ERROR finding `DEAUTHORIZED` is opened and `instagram.deauthorized` is audited. Idempotent (a repeat changes nothing, an unknown user is accepted and audited as unmatched).
  - **Data deletion**: the same matching; the row stays (publications and the audit trail point at it) but is **anonymized**: token destroyed, user name → `deleted-<id8>`, `instagram_account_id` → `deleted:<id>` (so the real account can be connected again as a new row), app-scoped id, type, scopes and last health cleared. The answer is `{ url, confirmation_code }` with a 12-character code (no look-alike characters) and the URL `<origin of IG_REDIRECT_URI>/data-deletion?code=…`. The code lives in the audit trail (`instagram.data_deletion_requested`, `data.confirmationCode`; `audit_events.entity_id` is a uuid so it does not go there); the status page looks it up there.
  - **Status page** `/data-deletion?code=`: public, English/Russian by cookie, says only that the request was handled and when, or that the code is unknown; a malformed code is rejected before the query.
  - `issued_at`/`expires` are not checked: both operations are idempotent, so a replayed request does nothing new.
- Not done: the privacy-policy page and the text of what we keep (B-13); entering the two URLs in the Meta app dashboard (B-11, runbook M5-10).
- Evidence: `instagram/callbacks.test.ts` (12: revoke by both ids, idempotency, unknown user, six kinds of invalid request for both callbacks with nothing changed, anonymization and re-connecting the same account, codes and the status answers, no name in the trail), `server/meta-callbacks.test.ts` (5), `auth/rules.test.ts` (the public paths).

## 2026-10-11 · Publish state machine [M5-06]
- Decision (`publishing/{publish-machine,safeguards,caption,settings}.ts`, `instagram/api.ts`, `ServiceContext.sleep`):
  - **`runPublication(ctx, {publicationId})`** runs a QUEUED/IN_PROGRESS publication from its persisted `step` (GUARDS → CREATE_CHILDREN → CREATE_PARENT → WAIT_READY → PUBLISH → FINALIZE) and returns `PUBLISHED | DRY_RUN_PASSED | FAILED | NOOP | IN_FLIGHT`. A publication that is finished, cancelled or still SCHEDULED is a **NOOP** (a second run of the same job does nothing: the idempotency drill). Starting a run counts an attempt (`attempt_count`), sets `trigger_run_id`, and moves a live post's variant SCHEDULED → PUBLISHING (`DISPATCH`; IMMEDIATE posts are PUBLISHING already). The account is the market's ACTIVE one, fixed on the first run.
  - **Meta calls are typed in `instagram/api.ts`** (create child/carousel container, container status, `media_publish`, media, recent media, publishing limit, follower count); the machine never writes an endpoint or a field name. Every call becomes a `publish_attempts` row (path only, redacted request/response, outcome, error code/subcode, fbtrace id, duration, run id). The HTTP wrapper now also **strips the query from any URL in a stored request**, so a presigned link is never persisted.
  - **Progress is saved after each unit of work**: one `ig_children_container_ids` update per child, the container id right after the parent is made. A retry after a rate limit reused the first child (checked: it is not made again). A child whose image Meta cannot download is retried **once with a fresh presigned link**, then the post FAILs as INVALID_MEDIA. Children are checked FINISHED before the parent is made (10 s pauses, six tries).
  - **Waiting** (`WAIT_READY`): status every 30 s for 3 minutes, then every 60 s, up to **10 minutes**, using `ctx.sleep` (Trigger.dev `wait.for` in jobs, so waiting costs no compute; tests advance a manual clock). `EXPIRED` → ids cleared and the steps run again, **once** (a second expiry fails); `ERROR` → INVALID_MEDIA; timeout → retryable. A **dry run stops here** as DRY_RUN_PASSED (audited), never calls `media_publish`, skips the quota call and leaves the draft untouched.
  - **The unsafe call** (`PUBLISH`): the guards run again; the container status is read; an unresolved AMBIGUOUS attempt or a PUBLISHED container goes to **reconciliation** (PUBLISHED → find our post among the last 25 media by whitespace-normalized caption and a time not before the attempt − 1 min → save the media id and mark the attempt OK; FINISHED → the earlier call did not take effect, publish; anything else, or no match → FAILED `MANUAL_CHECK_REQUIRED` with an ERROR finding and **no publish**). A young AMBIGUOUS row (< 2 min) of another run makes this run step aside (`IN_FLIGHT`); the row of this run is inserted **AMBIGUOUS, under a row lock, before the call is sent** and updated with the answer. A 5xx on `media_publish` is treated as unknown too (only an answer with a Graph error body is definitive).
  - **Safeguards** (`checkAllGuards`, 17 codes): mode `off` blocks everything, `dry_run_only` blocks live posts, the kill switch (`publishing.enabled`, missing = off) blocks live posts; the publication is not cancelled; the variant is SCHEDULED/PUBLISHING (dry run: APPROVED too); **the publication's version is the variant's `approved_version_id`**; the version verifies (`verifyApprovedVersion`); 2–10 slides of 1080×1350 ≤ 8 MB; **every stored image's SHA-256 equals the rendered one**; the account is ACTIVE, token valid and not expired, **belongs to the variant's market**, and is on **`INSTAGRAM_ACCOUNT_ALLOWLIST`** (an empty list allows nothing); no media id yet; the 24-hour allowance is not used up (fresh call); the composed caption (caption, blank line, hashtags) is ≤ 2,200 code points, ≤ 30 hashtags, ≤ 20 @ tags. The text sent is always the **snapshot's**, never the live variant's. A failed guard FAILs the post with `last_error.type = GUARD_<CODE>`, classification PERMANENT, and makes no Graph call.
  - **Failures**: 190 → account NEEDS_REAUTH (same helper as the refresh job) and FAILED; PERMISSION/PERMANENT/INVALID_MEDIA → FAILED; TRANSIENT/RATE_LIMIT/UNKNOWN (and an unexpected reply shape) → the run **throws a retryable error** and the publication stays IN_PROGRESS at its step, until `MAX_PUBLISH_ATTEMPTS` (5), then FAILED. A FAILED live post moves its variant to FAILED (`PUBLISH_FAILURE`), audited `publication.failed`. FINALIZE reads the permalink, time and follower count; if that fails the post is still PUBLISHED (permalink empty) with a WARN finding, because the post is already public.
  - `ServiceContext.sleep`; `isPublishingEnabled`; the fixtures' rendered slides now carry the real SHA-256 of their stored bytes.
- Not done here: creating IMMEDIATE and dry-run publications, the dispatcher, the job and `retryPublication` (M5-07); `setPublishingEnabled` and the header indicator (M5-08); the AI label (`is_ai_generated`, M5-12); a way for a person to resolve `MANUAL_CHECK_REQUIRED` other than checking the account and retrying (the retry reconciles again; if the post exists under another caption it stays blocked — a "mark as published with this link" action is a follow-up, M5-06a).
- Evidence: `publishing/publish-machine.test.ts` (32): the full happy path with the exact call sequence, arguments, attempt rows and audit; idempotent re-run; polling cadence; ten-minute timeout and resume; expiry recreated once; container ERROR; 190; rate limit with progress kept; fifth attempt FAILs; media-fetch re-presign; dropped answer reconciled; dead worker reconciled later with exactly one post; young foreign attempt → IN_FLIGHT; call that did not take effect published again; unfindable post → manual check and no blind retry; dry run; mode off / dry_run_only / kill switch / allowlist / wrong-market account / no account / expired token / changed version / draft state / replaced image / wrong size / long caption / allowance used up / cancelled / text from the snapshot.

## 2026-10-11 · Dispatcher, publish job, publish now, dry run and retry [M5-07]
- Decision (`publishing/{dispatcher,queue,retry,schedule}.ts`, handlers `publish-dispatcher` and `publish-content`, `jobs/src/tasks/publish-*.ts`, `db/src/test-db-postgres.ts`, CI job `concurrency`):
  - **J9 `publish-dispatcher`** (cron `* * * * *`, no retry: the next minute is the retry): with the environment `off` it does nothing; in `dry_run_only` mode or with the kill switch off it dispatches **dry runs only** (a live post waits, it does not fail). It claims up to 10 due SCHEDULED publications, oldest first, in one transaction with **`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`**, marks them QUEUED with the market's ACTIVE account, then starts J10 for each with the publication's own idempotency key and `concurrencyKey` = the account (one publication at a time per Instagram account), and stores the run id. A job that cannot be started puts the post back to SCHEDULED. **Recovery**: a QUEUED publication with no run id after 2 minutes (the runner was down) is started; a QUEUED/IN_PROGRESS one untouched for 30 minutes is started again with a recovery key for that half hour (the old key stays taken by a run that already ended); both are skipped while live posting is blocked.
  - **J10 `publish-content`**: runs `runPublication`; queue `instagram-publish`, 30 minutes, **5 attempts, 30 s → 10 min**. A FAILED post returns normally (the person retries it); a retryable failure is rethrown; the machine's own attempt counter ends it after the fifth. `handlerTask`/`cronHandlerTask` accept a `retry` policy.
  - **Run lease** (found while writing the race test): two runs on one publication could both walk the steps and one could write a stale step. A run now steps aside (`IN_FLIGHT`) when another run id has saved progress within **3 minutes** (`RUN_ALIVE_MS`; the machine saves at least every poll and a call times out in 30 s); a run that stops with a retryable error **lets go** (`trigger_run_id = null`), so a retry with any run id continues at once; a Trigger.dev retry keeps its run id. `finalize` is idempotent under a lock (a second run does not publish the variant twice).
  - **`schedulePublication` now does `IMMEDIATE` and dry runs**: it refuses up front, with wording fit for the UI, when the environment is off / only allows dry runs / the kill switch is off / the market has no usable, allowlisted account; a live post needs an APPROVED draft (draft → PUBLISHING), a dry run an APPROVED or SCHEDULED one (the draft is untouched, any number of times). The publication is QUEUED with its account and the job is started after the commit; if the runner is down the publication is kept and found as an orphan.
  - **`retryPublication`** (FAILED only): checks the environment and account now, `retry_no + 1`, key `pub:{id}:{n}`, attempts back to 0, error cleared, **step and containers kept** (a different account starts from the beginning), the draft FAILED → PUBLISHING, audit with the previous error, job started with the new key. A post that failed with `MANUAL_CHECK_REQUIRED` is reconciled again, never published blindly.
  - **Real-Postgres tests**: `createPostgresTestDb(TEST_POSTGRES_URL)` makes a throw-away database (all migrations) and extra connection pools. `publishing/concurrency.postgres.test.ts` runs six dispatchers draining 30 due posts (each taken once, 30 distinct keys), eight parallel schedules of one draft (exactly one succeeds), and four workers on one publication (exactly one post, three step aside). CI got a **`concurrency` job with a `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` service container**. **These three tests have not been run yet**: this machine has no Postgres and Docker is not running; they are skipped without `TEST_POSTGRES_URL`. The same logic is covered sequentially on PGlite (claim twice, parallel promises on one connection, the lease, the race of two runs), which does not prove lock behavior.
- Evidence: `publishing/dispatch.test.ts` ( dispatch order and fields, no double claim, the limit of 10, mode off / kill switch / dry_run_only, runner down, orphan and stuck recovery, publish now and its refusals, dry runs, retry in all its forms, and an end-to-end run of the real handlers through the inline runner: publish now → PUBLISHED; scheduled → dispatcher → PUBLISHED; a repeated job → no third post), `publish-machine.test.ts` (36, with the lease cases), `jobs.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · Environment guards, kill switch and header indicator [M5-08]
- Decision (`publishing/kill-switch.ts`, `components/instagram/publishing-switch.tsx`, `components/shell/publishing-indicator.tsx`, `lib/publishing-indicator.ts`, `(app)/layout.tsx`, `/settings/instagram`):
  - The guards themselves were built where they are used: **mode** and **allowlist** in the safeguards (M5-06), the dispatcher and the "publish now" checks (M5-07). `INSTAGRAM_PUBLISH_MODE`: `off` blocks everything, `dry_run_only` blocks live posts, `live` needs the switch. **An empty `INSTAGRAM_ACCOUNT_ALLOWLIST` allows no account**, so a fresh environment publishes nowhere until its test accounts are listed (dev must never hold production ids, 09 §9.8).
  - **`setPublishingEnabled({enabled, reason})`** (owner only, reason 1–500 characters): writes `publishing.enabled` with `updated_by`, audits `publishing.enabled` / `publishing.disabled` with who, why, the previous value and the environment's mode. The setting was already one of the allowed `app_settings` keys; this action is the one with a reason (the generic `setAppSetting` stays for the settings screen). Missing = off, and the seed starts it off. Switching off: the dispatcher holds live posts (they stay SCHEDULED), a post already on its way **fails its guards before `media_publish`** with "Publishing is switched off" and can be retried; dry runs are not affected.
  - **`getPublishingStatus`** → `{mode, enabled, live, allowlist}`. **Header**: a small badge on every page, always visible: *Off here* (environment `off`), *Paused* (switch off in a live environment), *Dry runs only*, *Live*; it links to the switch. A **warning banner** appears only for *Paused* (in an environment that cannot publish at all it would be noise). `/settings/instagram` has the switch card: the state, the explanation, what the environment limits (and the allowed account ids, which are not secret), a confirmation dialog that requires a reason, a read-only note for editors and chefs.
- Evidence: `publishing/kill-switch.test.ts` (4: status for every mode, on/off with audit data, owners only and the reason, the dispatcher holding and a queued post failing with no Graph call), `components/instagram/publishing-switch.test.tsx` (the dialog for both directions, the server's refusal shown, non-owners, Russian, the four header states), `lib/publishing-indicator.test.ts`; mode `off` / `dry_run_only` / unknown-account cases are in `publish-machine.test.ts` and `dispatch.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-11 · Publish UX: panel, drawer, retry [M5-09]
- Decision (`publishing/details.ts`, `components/publishing/{publish-panel,publication-drawer}.tsx`, `lib/publication-dto.ts`, `getPublication` action, catalogs `publish.*`):
  - **Data**: `getPublicationDetails` (the publication, its market, the approved hook as title, the account's user name, the error and **every call in order**, `canRetry` for FAILED, `canCancel` for SCHEDULED/QUEUED) and `listVariantPublications` (per draft: the newest real post, cancelled ones excluded, and the newest dry run). Errors cross to the UI as a **kind** (`AUTH`, `INVALID_MEDIA_FETCH`, `MANUAL_CHECK_REQUIRED`, `GUARD_<CODE>`, …), not as English text: the catalogs hold, in both languages, a title and a "what to do" for each of the 25 kinds; the raw message, code, subcode and trace id sit under "technical details". Dates cross as ISO strings (`toPublicationDto`).
  - **Review screen** gets a "Publishing" panel under the actions for drafts that are APPROVED, SCHEDULED, PUBLISHING, PUBLISHED or FAILED: the status line (with the time in the market's zone), the readable error for a failed post, the last dry run, and for owners and editors **Publish now** (a confirmation: it goes live and cannot be taken back from here), **Schedule…** (the calendar's dialog), **Dry run** (no confirmation; any number of times; opens the details), **Retry** (FAILED), **Move or call off** (SCHEDULED), a link to the post once published, and **Details**. A PUBLISHING draft refreshes the page every 5 seconds. Chefs see the status and the details, not the buttons. The server's refusal (kill switch, no account, …) shows inside the confirmation.
  - **Drawer** (a sheet): status badge, step in words, account, times in the market's zone, attempts and retries, the error block, Retry / Open in review / Open on Instagram, and the timeline of calls (time, step, path, Graph code and subcode, outcome, HTTP status, duration). It reloads when it opens and every 5 seconds while the post is QUEUED/IN_PROGRESS. The calendar's post dialog got a **Details** button that opens it.
  - `retryPublication` and `getPublication` are actions (owner/editor; read for all roles).
- Not done here: the live walkthrough with the mock Graph API and screenshots (next commit, the E2E specs for publishing); a "mark as published" action for `MANUAL_CHECK_REQUIRED` (M5-06a).
- Evidence: `publishing/details.test.ts` (5), `components/publishing/publish-panel.test.tsx` (14: every status's buttons, the confirmation and its cancel, the server's refusal, dry run, retry, published link, scheduled post, read-only, Russian, the drawer's error words and timeline, retry, links, load failure).

Nothing is written to the database; no token appears in the report (tested).

The log in the runbook is empty on purpose; nothing in this entry is a result from a real account. The script itself is exercised by `instagram/smoke.test.ts` (10: dry run with no post and no database writes, live run with the second-publish probe and no token in the log, a mock that accepts the second publish, allowlist / off / dry_run_only refusals with zero calls, a refused token, a refused presigned link with its advice, a container that never finishes, real slide dimensions).

## 2026-10-11 · AI imagery disclosure [M5-12, V-16]
- Decision (migration `0008_market_ai_disclosure`, `visuals/disclosure.ts`, `ApprovedSnapshot.disclosure`, `templates` AI badge, `publishing/caption.ts`, publish machine, `DisclosureNote`, market form):
  - **Setting per market**: `markets.ai_imagery_disclosure` = `NONE` (default) | `CAPTION_NOTE` | `SLIDE_BADGE` | `PLATFORM_LABEL`, with a check constraint. Only an **owner** can change it (`updateMarket`, audited as a changed field; a legal decision, B-13). The market form has the select with plain-language options. The default stays NONE until B-13 decides; the code is ready for whichever they choose.
  - **What counts**: any slide whose image slot holds a READY generated image (`visual_assets.is_ai_generated`). Nothing in the data says whether a picture is photo-realistic, so the conservative reading applies (every generated image); counsel may narrow it later by adding a field.
  - **Decided at approval, kept in the snapshot** (`planDisclosure` → `disclosure` in `ApprovedSnapshot`, part of the content hash): what is published is what the reviewer saw, and a setting changed later does not change an approved post. The effects: **CAPTION_NOTE** appends a note in the market's language (es: "Imágenes generadas con IA.", en: "Images created with AI.", ru; English for any other language) as its own paragraph between the caption and the hashtags (`composeCaption`; the 2,200-character guard counts it). **PLATFORM_LABEL** sets Instagram's **`is_ai_generated=true`** on the **carousel container only** (V-16, available since 2026-06-22; the label cannot be added after publishing). **SLIDE_BADGE** draws a small "AI" badge (top right, inline style so no template's HTML or golden image changes) on the slides that carry a generated image: the badge is part of the **render**, so the render input hash includes the badged slide ids (only when there are any, so older renders keep their hash). Changing the setting after a render makes that render stale, and approval waits for a new one (tested).
  - **The reviewer sees it**: `VariantView.disclosure` is the plan for the draft as it stands; the review screen shows a note under the caption, and the approve dialog repeats it ("A note is added to the caption: …", "The slides that have one carry an “AI” badge", "Instagram's “AI info” label is requested"). Texts in both catalogs.
- Not decided here (B-13): whether the platform label alone is enough under the EU AI Act transparency rules or a caption note is needed as well; which markets get which setting. The G2 record has a row for it.
- Evidence: `visuals/disclosure.test.ts` (7), `content/variants/disclosure.test.ts` (6: NONE adds nothing, snapshot with note verifies, label request, no generated image → nothing, the reviewer's preview, badge hash and per-slide flag, stale render blocks approval), `publishing/publish-machine.test.ts` (note between caption and hashtags, label on the carousel container only, the note counted in the limit), `localization/update-market.test.ts` (owner only), `templates` framework test (badge only when asked), `components/review/disclosure-note.test.tsx`.

## 2026-10-11 · Web database pool in production [M5, found on the first live deploy]
- Context: on the first production deploy with M5 several pages (`/content/drafts`, the review page) failed at random with "Failed query" on trivial statements, and loaded on the next try. The Vercel logs show only the statement, not the cause.
- Decision: in production the web app's pool is **2 connections per instance** (5 elsewhere). Hypothesis, not yet proven: every serverless instance has its own pool and a page makes several requests at once (the sidebar prefetches), so 5 per instance exhaust the session pooler's client limit. A small pool queues instead of failing. Check after the deploy: the count of `Failed query` errors in the Vercel runtime logs; if they continue, look at the Supabase pooler's client limit and at logging the error's `cause` (Drizzle wraps it).
- Also: the review page crashed once on `PUBLISHABLE_STATUSES.includes` (a constant exported from a `use client` file and read by a server component; fixed in #79). Unit tests cannot see that boundary: a value that a server component needs must not live in a `use client` file.

## 2026-10-12 · Web database pool is released after each request [M5, live deploy]
- Context: the hypothesis of the entry above was half right. The pool of 2 never applied: Vercel runs with `APP_ENV=staging`, and the check was `=== "production"`. And the real cause of the "Failed query" errors was measured on the session pooler (port 5432): `EMAXCONNSESSION, max clients reached in session mode, pool_size: 15`, with all 15 backends idle for over a minute although the client has `idle_timeout: 20`. A serverless instance is frozen between requests, so the pool's idle timer cannot run and the connections stay open, each holding a pooler slot until the instance is thrown away. A switch to the transaction pooler (6543) removed the slot limit but made single queries hang for the full 300 s function limit (a backend stuck in `ClientRead`); it did not reproduce from a laptop in 72 concurrent queries on either port, so it is specific to the Vercel runtime and was not explained. Reverted to 5432.
- Decision: `isServerless` is `staging` or `production`; there the pool is 2 connections, and `requestContext` calls `releaseDbAfterRequest()`, which registers `after()` and closes the pool when the last request that used it is done (a counter, so overlapping requests on one instance share the pool). The next request opens a new one. The cost is one connection handshake per quiet request; the gain is that no idle instance holds a slot. Trigger.dev tasks keep using the transaction pooler (6543): their runs are short-lived and a render through it succeeded.
- Not done: `cause` of Drizzle's "Failed query" is still not logged; do that before the next database mystery.

## 2026-10-12 · Menu links do not prefetch; the cause of a database error is logged [M5, live deploy]
- Context: after the pool was released per request, a burst of six page requests at once still failed with "Failed query" in about 300 ms (a fast refusal, consistent with the session pooler's 15 client slots). The bursts come from the app itself: every page is dynamic, so a prefetch of a menu link is a full server render with its queries, and the menu has about 15 links. The measurement that the pooler's idle backends belong to frozen instances (previous entry) was too strong: an idle backend in `pg_stat_activity` can also be a pooled backend without a client, so the number of clients is not observable from the database.
- Decision: `prefetch={false}` on the sidebar links. `onRequestError` logs the `cause` of the thrown error (code and message only, never the parameters), because Drizzle's "Failed query" hides it and the Vercel log showed only that. Do this before any further guess about the pooler.

## 2026-10-12 · Carousel style: content-writer@4 and visual-director@3 eval [style, PR #87]
- Context: the accepted carousel style (`docs/design/carousel-style.md`) needs `**…**` emphasis in slot text (`content-writer@4`) and cut-out photos on white (`visual-director@3`). Plan 07 §7.5: a new version goes live only after an eval run with no regression.
- Run: `pnpm eval --provider live --judge` on the synthetic set (3 cases × es-ES and en), the same day, once on `main` (p1.2.0, writer@3) and once on this branch (p1.3.0, writer@4); model claude-opus-5-5. Cost: $2.49 (baseline) + $2.28 (new).
- Result (baseline → new): cases meeting expectations 3/3 → 3/3; completion 100 % → 100 %; schema-valid runs 97 % → 100 %; citation coverage 100 % → 100 %; numeric fidelity 100 % → 100 %; differentiation FAIL 0 → 0; critic quality 4.66 → 4.67; judge factual / localization / voice 2.50 / 3.67 / 3.83 → 3.00 / 4.00 / 4.00; judge unsupported claims 12 → 13; cost per idea $0.83 → $0.76; time per idea 220 s → 199 s. **No regression.** The markers appear in slide text as intended (for example "**1:2 ratio**", "**15 min**") and nowhere in hook, caption or CTA.
- The low factual score of `buckwheat-lid` (1/5 in both runs) and most of the "unsupported claims" in both runs are a harness defect, not the writer's: the eval judge gets only `title`, `claim` and `explanation` of a card (`evals/src/runner.ts`), not `procedure` or `timings`, so the card's 1:2 ratio and 15/10-minute times look invented to it although the code check of numbers passes. The judge's numbers are therefore only comparable between runs, not absolute.

- Context: "Regenerate pictures" on the English variant "Rescue grains that came out too wet" (2026-10-13 08:20Z) ran `visual-director@4` in one attempt ($0.0775; about $0.37 with its 7 pictures). The brief validator blocks too few accents and a serving suggestion on a step picture (one repair); briefs without colour fields are not checked. Footer handle and page number get a halo in the page colour (templates 2.0.1; goldens unchanged).
