## 2026-10-13 · Video provider (Higgsfield) and V-24 partly verified [M8-02]
- Context: M8-02 adds `lib/src/providers/video` (types, Higgsfield adapter, fake, factory, estimator) and `pnpm video:smoke`.
- Verified by real calls on 2026-10-13 (about 25 shots over the test): authorization `Key <id>:<secret>` on `api.higgsfield.ai`; `POST /files/generate-upload-url` (answer: `upload_url`, `upload_headers`, `public_url`) then a PUT without credentials; `POST /<model>/image-to-video` with an `Idempotency-Key` header (answer `request_id`, `status_url`, `cancel_url`); `GET /requests/{id}/status` with `queued`, `in_progress`, `completed` (`video.url`), and `failed`, `nsfw`, `canceled` as terminal states from the docs; the clip downloads from a CDN URL without credentials. A request can sit in the queue for 10 minutes or more (it happened twice); a resubmission worked.
- Not verified: the `402` mapping for a low balance (taken from common practice, covered by a mocked test only); rate limits (none found in the docs); how long the vendor keeps files (a third-party guide says at least 7 days), so the adapter returns the bytes and the job stores them in R2 right away.
- Terms (V-24, from Higgsfield's help center, not the Terms text itself, which could not be fetched): Higgsfield does not claim ownership of outputs and does not restrict commercial use (it cites Terms of Use §4.4), API outputs included; it keeps a licence to use content to run the service and train models until the content or account is deleted; it may refuse or remove content that breaks its policies. No watermark was visible in the test clips. **Open:** read §4.4 and the training licence in the Terms of Use directly and decide whether the owner accepts the training licence for product pictures; confirm the rate limits with support if volume grows.
- Decision: adapter split into `submit` and `waitFor` (the handle holds request id, model, seconds and resolution and no secret), so a crashed or timed-out run resumes without paying twice; `failed`, `canceled` and `nsfw` are permanent errors, a timeout is transient and carries the request id; costs are list-price estimates (an upper bound; the measured Kling and Seedance shots matched the rates after the shown discounts). `HIGGSFIELD_API_KEY` is optional in the live `ai` env so existing deployments keep loading; without it the provider refuses every call with a clear message and the Reel button can say why it is off.
- Impact on plan: none; wiring into `ServiceContext` moves to M8-06.

## 2026-10-14 · Reel data model, migration 0009 [M8-04]
- Context: plan 18 §18.3 proposed `reel_shots` keyed by `(variant, scene, prompt hash)`. The owner's decision that the Spanish Reel reuses the English shots (Q-10) needs the shots to be found across variants.
- Decision: `reel_shots` is scoped to the **brand** and unique on `(brand_id, prompt_hash)` among PENDING, RUNNING and READY (a failed or blocked shot may be recreated); the Reel that ordered a shot is only recorded in `content_variant_id`, and scenes refer to shots by `shotId`. `reel_scripts` (versioned per variant, jsonb hook options and scenes) and `reel_renders` (like `carousel_renders`, with `input_hash` and a required `storage_key` when READY) are declared in `schema/content.ts` next to `content_variants` because of the module cycle; `content_variants` gets `source_variant_id` (only for `format = 'REEL'`, a check) and `current_reel_render_id`. `generation_stage` gets `REEL_DIRECTION` and `REEL_SHOT`. Statuses and shapes: `reel_shot_status` enum, Zod shapes in `db/src/json/reels.ts` (a card has at most 6 words, a scene is 3 to 5 beats on a half-beat grid, nine emotions).
- Not done: the REEL entries of the variant state machine (guards and effects that look at the current render) wait for M8-06 and M8-09; `prompts/src/define.ts` gets `REEL_DIRECTION` with M8-03; owner-supplied audio is not modelled until M8-08.
- Evidence: schema tests (RLS, one live Reel per idea and market next to the carousel, source variant only on a Reel, script versions, render uniqueness and the READY file check, current Reel render, live-shot guard and reuse, running and ready checks, trigger-kept `updated_at`, new stages, JSON shapes); `pnpm test` in `db`.
- Impact on plan: 18 §18.3 and 04 (section 0009) updated.

## 2026-10-14 · Hook library and the Reel director [M2-12b, M8-03]
- Context: the owner supplied a public list of 100 hook headlines. The carousels already have a `hook_type` taxonomy (10 codes) that `market-adapter` picks from and analytics reports on, but the prompts only saw the code and its label.
- Decision: one library for both formats (`docs/design/hook-library.md`, `prompts/src/shared/hook-library.ts`): patterns, best angles and example lines per `hook_type`, plus banned patterns per language (invented experience, guarantees, conspiracy framing, time promises, empty hype). Reels reuse the carousel codes instead of a second taxonomy, so one report compares both formats. New prompt versions `market-adapter@3` and `content-writer@5` use it, and `validateHookLine` blocks banned patterns and bare numbers that no card supports (the HOOK slide cites no card, so the numeric check did not reach it). `reel-director@1` directs a Reel from the approved carousel: three hook options of three different hook types, 6–9 scenes on the 120 BPM grid, validated by `validateReelScript` with one repair. `ReelHookOption.formula` was renamed `hookType` and got `claimSlideIds` (JSON column, no migration, no data yet). The Spanish Reel takes the English script as `baseScript` and writes only the cards.
- Run (2026-10-14, claude-opus-5-5, synthetic set, 3 cases × es-ES and en, `--judge`): once with `market-adapter@2` + `content-writer@4` (p1.6.0) and once with `@3` + `@5`. Cost about $2.49 + $2.13. Result (before → after): cases meeting expectations 2/3 → 3/3 (before, `buckwheat-lid` failed on the forbidden word "always" in en); completion, schema-valid runs, citation coverage and numeric fidelity 100 % → 100 %; differentiation FAIL 0 → 0; critic quality 4.72 → 4.62 (six drafts, within the run-to-run spread seen on 2026-10-12); judge factual / localization / voice 2.83 / 3.83 / 3.67 → 3.00 / 4.00 / 4.00; judge unsupported claims 19 → 11; cost per idea $0.83 → $0.71; time per idea 220 s → 189 s. The hooks read as more concrete (a stated cause or condition instead of a general warning). The judge's low factual score has the same harness cause as on 2026-10-12 (it sees only title, claim and explanation of a card), so only the direction counts.
- Decision: activate `market-adapter@3` and `content-writer@5` (p1.7.0). The `validateHookLine` check is part of the pipeline from the same release.
- `reel-director@1` (live, 3 synthetic posts, $0.33): 3/3 valid scripts, one needed the repair; 12.75–13 s, 6–7 scenes, three different hook types per script, no notes left. The owner reviewed the three scripts from the sheet (`pnpm eval:reels` output `*-reels-live-review.md`) and accepted them on 2026-10-14: V-29 closed. The review was of the text of the scripts; no video exists yet.
- Evidence: unit tests for the library, both prompt versions, `validateHookLine`, `validateReelScript`, `directReel` (success, repair, failure after the repair) and the Reel eval; the fake `pnpm eval` passes with the new carousel versions switched on; the whole suite is green.
- Impact on plan: task M2-12b added, M8-03 depends on it; 18 and `reel-style.md` link the library.

## 2026-10-14 · Reel assembly renderer [M8-05]
- Context: the director gives a script and the provider gives shots; something has to turn them into the accepted Reel format (`docs/design/reel-style.md`).
- Decision: `modules/src/reels/render/` assembles a Reel from local files in one ffmpeg run (one filter graph: cut, lift the grey cast to white with `colorlevels` 0.9, a 907 × 1613 box flush right on white or full bleed, the card's PNG with a 0.12 s fade, hard cuts), H.264 High, yuv420p, 30 fps, AAC (a silent track when there is no audio), faststart. The flash opener takes four 0.22 s slices at 0.6 s into four later shots, under the hook card. Cards are drawn by the existing Chromium with Inter Tight 800, the largest size from 128 down to 92 px that fits three lines in the 780 px column, and measured against the safe zone; a card that needs less than 92 px is a QA warning. Checks: format, black frames (`blackdetect`), motion in the first second (`tblend` + `signalstats`, threshold 0.1, provisional), card positions; every finding is a warning in `ReelQaReport`, none stops the render. The input hash covers the scenes' cards, beats and layout, each shot's content hash and start, the audio and `RENDERER_VERSION`. The domain `reels` may depend on `visuals` (Chromium launcher).
- Evidence: unit tests (cut list and beat grid, flash opener, filter graph, hash, QA parsers), Chromium card tests including all 21 cards of the synthetic posts, and real ffmpeg tests on synthetic clips with five golden frames. A demo Reel made from the accepted Higgsfield bulgur clips and the bulgur script: 14.93 s, 1080 × 1920, h264 + aac, no warnings, first-second motion 8.8.
- Not verified: V-27 (ffmpeg and Chromium together on Trigger.dev production machines, memory and time of a 20 s render); the golden frames were made on macOS and use no fonts, but ffmpeg builds may differ slightly (the tolerance is 0.4 % of pixels).
- Impact on plan: M8-06 must add the ffmpeg build extension to `jobs/trigger.config.ts` and measure V-27; M8-08 supplies the audio file.

## 2026-10-14 · Reel generation: service, job J23, kill switch [M8-06]
- Context: the director gives a script, the provider gives shots, the renderer gives the video; something has to run them in order, pay for nothing twice, and stop when the owner says so.
- Decision: `generateReel` (job `generate-reel`, queue `render`, `large-1x`, 25 minutes) runs director → shots → assembly for one Reel variant and keeps its place in `content_variants.pipeline_state` (`reel:{variantId}:{requestId}`, the stage, the script id, the director's cost). A shot is found by the hash of model, resolution, seconds, prompt and start frame, so a rerun, a retry and an identical shot in another script cost nothing; a RUNNING shot is waited for again instead of resubmitted; a refusal (moderation, a failed job) is retried once with a still prompt ("a soft light shifts over the subject"), the refused row is BLOCKED or FAILED, and a second refusal ends the run. A transient error (a vendor timeout) is rethrown and the variant stays GENERATING; anything else ends it as DRAFT with the flag `GENERATION_FAILED` and the job returns normally. The services behind the button are `estimateReelCost` (no model call: 7 shots × 3 s × the model's list rate plus about $0.10 for the director, about $2.2 on Wan) and `requestReel`, which needs `reels.enabled` (a new `app_settings` key, seeded false, missing means off) and a confirmed estimate within $0.02 of the current one, creates or reuses the Reel variant of the post (a Reel in review may be generated again; one being made, approved or scheduled may not) and queues J23. The Reel posts with the carousel's caption, hashtags and call to action. Every vendor call is a `generation_runs` row with stage `REEL_SHOT`. `ServiceContext` has `video`, wired in the jobs runtime and the CLI.
- Not done: the web runtime does not get `video` until M8-07 needs it; there is no screen for `reels.enabled` yet (set it with `pnpm rc setting reels.enabled true`, which lists the settings without arguments, or the `setAppSetting` action); a market's script is not reused by the other market (task M8-06a): each market's Reel pays for its own shots; "change hook" swaps the two cards without running the validators again (the last-card echo of the new hook is not checked); audio is none until M8-08.
- Not verified: V-27 on Trigger.dev production (the ffmpeg build extension is added in `jobs/trigger.config.ts`; apt ffmpeg is on PATH); a live run on a real post.
- Evidence: unit tests for estimate, shots (reuse, resume, fallback, concurrency), request (switch, estimate, variant creation, guards) and the job with fakes; one test of the whole job with real ffmpeg and Chromium that ends in a clean 14.9 s 1080 × 1920 mp4 in storage.
- Impact on plan: M8-06a added; 06 J23 as planned.

## 2026-10-14 · The Reel card on the review screen [M8-07]
- Context: Reels can be generated (M8-06) but only from code; the owner needs the button, the price, the progress and the video where the draft is.
- Decision: a `ReelCard` in each market's column of the review screen, driven by `getReelView` (one plain object: the switch, why the button is off, the price of each model, and for an existing Reel its stage, script, scenes with their shots, video links, QA warnings and cost). The estimate dialog shows three models, re-reads the price from the server on a change, and sends exactly the price the owner saw (`confirmEstimateUsd`). Nothing is charged before that. **Change hook** writes the next script version with the other option's two cards and runs J23 from the shots on: every shot is reused, no director call, only a new video (free). **Redo shot** writes the next version with that scene's `shotAttempt` raised (it enters the shot hash, so it is a new shot), pays for that one shot after a confirmation with its price, and rebuilds the video. Both use `scriptId` in the job payload. Only an owner or an editor may call the four actions (they spend money). The review poller treats a Reel being made like a draft being written, and a video being rebuilt like a render in progress. Reel variants are filtered out of every carousel query (review bundle, drafts and their tab counts, work-queue counters, the idea's variants, the pipeline context, the draft request) so a Reel never shows as an empty carousel column; they join the queues with M8-09.
- Not done: the published post screen is still a placeholder, so the card is not on it; approve and reject (M8-09); an E2E spec (M8-07a); a screen for the `reels.enabled` switch (use `pnpm rc setting`).
- Evidence: 17 component tests (the dialog, the confirmed price, the model re-read, every blocked reason, the progress and the slow message, a failed Reel, the video, the hook change, the shot redo, no change for an approved Reel or for a non-owner), service tests for the view, the hook change (free: no shot, no director, one new render) and the shot redo (exactly one new shot, a second redo another shot), the i18n parity test, and a Chrome rendering of every state of the card.
- Impact on plan: M8-07a added; M8-09 puts Reels into the queues and adds approve and reject.

## 2026-10-14 · Placeholder beat and the Reel player's CSP [M8-08, M8-07]
- Context: the first Reel made through the app was silent, and the owner could not play it in the card (the downloaded file played).
- Cause of the player: the Content-Security-Policy had no `media-src`, so the `<video>` fell back to `default-src 'self'` and the browser refused the presigned R2 link. Fix: `media-src 'self' blob: https://*.r2.cloudflarestorage.com` (PR #104), with a test.
- Decision (audio, RL-7): `generate-reel` makes the placeholder beat in code (a port of the spike's synthesizer, no licence to hold): 120 BPM, silent-first but with a beat: kick and clap from the second scene, a bass and hat pulse under the hook, a pop on every cut, an impact before the peak scene, a riser out of the flash opener. It is deterministic (seed 7), as long as the video, ends with a 0.35 s fade, and is soft-clipped to a usable level: −15.3 LUFS and a true peak of −1.1 dBTP by ebur128 (without the clipping it sat at −20.6 LUFS, with a drive of 2.4 at −11.9 LUFS and +1.0 dBTP, so 1.4 was chosen). The beat's hash is part of the render's input hash, so a Reel made before this change is rebuilt (for free, with "Use this hook" or a new request) and gets its sound. `audio_kind = PLACEHOLDER_BEAT`.
- Not done: owner-supplied licensed tracks (Q-08) and their upload; Instagram's own music library cannot be added through the API.
- Evidence: tests for the schedule (cuts, kick and clap entry, the impact, the riser), the WAV, determinism, level, and a loudness check by ffmpeg.

## 2026-10-15 · Reels in review and publishing; V-25 checked [M8-09]
- Context: a Reel could be generated and watched (M8-06, M8-07) but not approved, scheduled or posted; Reels were kept out of the queues.
- V-25 (read 2026-10-15 in Meta's docs: content publishing guide, `POST /<IG_ID>/media` reference, IG Container, `content_publishing_limit`, `media_publish`, error codes): `media_type=REELS` with `video_url` on a public server (Meta fetches it with cURL, US-ASCII URL only); MOV/MP4 with the moov atom first, H.264 or HEVC, 4:2:0, closed GOP, AAC up to 48 kHz, 23–60 fps, at most 1920 px wide, 3 s–15 min, at most 300 MB, up to 25 Mbps; 9:16 recommended; `share_to_feed` (Reels only); `cover_url` / `thumb_offset`; `trial_params.graduation_strategy` `MANUAL` or `SS_PERFORMANCE` (M8-11); `is_ai_generated` is listed for every media type except carousel children, so it applies to Reels (inferred: Reels are not named); the resumable upload is documented "only for Facebook Login for Business" apps, so `video_url` is our path; a container lives 24 h and `status` carries the error subcode next to ERROR; Meta's own docs disagree on the 24-hour limit (100 in one place, 50 in the reference), so the code keeps reading `content_publishing_limit`. Our encoder profile is inside every limit.
- Decision: (1) An approved version of a Reel points at its **reel render** (migration 0010: `content_variant_versions.carousel_render_id` nullable, new `reel_render_id`, exactly one set). The snapshot gets `format: "REEL"` and `reel` (render, script id and version, hook, the mp4's key, size, SHA-256, length and size in pixels, audio kind, the cards); a carousel's snapshot is unchanged. (2) `approveVariant` branches on the format; a Reel has the carousel's guards (lock version, current video = newest script, blocking flags, offer if commercial, checklist), its knowledge list is the cards cited by the carousel slides its claims come from. (3) Reject, request changes, resubmit and un-approve are the generic ones; the Reel card offers only those and the publishing actions (no field edits; a Reel changes by a new hook or a redone shot). (4) The AI disclosure of a Reel always applies (every shot is generated): CAPTION_NOTE gets "Video created with AI." in the market's language, SLIDE_BADGE falls back to the caption note (a video cannot get a badge afterwards), PLATFORM_LABEL sends `is_ai_generated`. (5) The publisher's REELS branch: guards check the stored mp4 against the approved size and SHA-256 and Instagram's limits (`VIDEO_INVALID`, `VIDEO_CHANGED`); no children; one `REELS` container with a fresh presigned link (2 h) and `share_to_feed=true`; wait for FINISHED up to 15 minutes; a video Meta could not fetch gets one new container, then fails; the dry run stops at FINISHED. The container status is now read with `status_code,status`, and the subcode in `status` is kept (so a fetch failure is recognised). (6) Reels join the draft queue (with their format and a Reel badge), the work-queue counters, the calendar (a Reel badge, no thumbnail) and its "approved, not scheduled" list. (7) `pnpm ig:smoke --reel [--video f.mp4]` (a synthetic 6 s ffmpeg clip by default) and `pnpm rc g3-check`; runbook `docs/runbooks/reels.md`; gate record `docs/decisions/G3.md` (not decided).
- Not done: V-26 (a Reel dry run on the test account, needs 0010 applied, the code deployed and @chef.skk reconnected); the G3 decision and the first live Reel (owner); `trial_params` (M8-11); a cover image (`cover_url`, Instagram takes the first frame); the published-post screen still has no Reel card (M8-07 open item). A carousel edited after its Reel was requested does not update the Reel's caption (the Reel keeps the caption copied at request time).
- Evidence: an end-to-end test at the modules root (request → generate with fakes → approve → snapshot → dry run on the mock Graph API → publish → calendar → `g3-check`; stale video refused; a changed file refused; one container remade after a fetch error; reject frees the slot; un-approve and approve again as version 2); smoke tests for the Reel path; ReelCard tests for approve, the Reel wording of blockers, no "render again", and the publishing panel; the existing suites unchanged.
- Impact on plan: none beyond M8-09; migration 0010 must be applied before the code is deployed.

## 2026-10-15 · Reel card end to end, the Reels switch, and a frozen review page [M8-07a]
- Context: the Reel card was tested with mocked actions only; nothing had run the whole path (switch, estimate, job, assembly, hook change, shot redo, approval, calendar) through the real web server, and `reels.enabled` could only be set with `pnpm rc setting`.
- Decision: (1) The E2E server gets the pieces a Reel needs: the scripted model answers `reel-director` (`modules/src/ai/scripted-reel.ts`, built from the slide ids, picture ids and hook types of the request, no numbers in the cards; a unit test runs it through `validateReelScript` for the three synthetic posts and for E2E-shaped carousels, one and several slides, es and en), a video provider `createE2eVideo` (the fake provider with a real 4 s 720 × 1280 mp4 per shot, made by ffmpeg, a different hue for each) and `serverVideo()` in the web runtime, used by the in-process job runner only. The assembly is the real one. (2) The switch of Reel generation is on **Settings → AI** (`ReelsSwitch`, one click, owners only, `setAppSetting` and its audit event; the placeholder page gained `children`). (3) `apps/web/e2e/reels.e2e.ts` runs the whole path in 1.8 minutes and puts the screenshots of the ready and the approved Reel in `test-results`. (4) The database of the E2E run is the shared dev project, which a deployment uses as well, so the spec never switches a live `reels.enabled` off: if it is on, only the screen is checked; if it is off, the spec switches it on and puts it back. The spec's owner user (`e2e-owner@content.test`) is set inactive at the end. Its cleanup also removes Reel videos, scripts, shots and their model runs.
- **Bug found and fixed:** the review page stayed on "Making the Reel" for good (twice, 7 minutes each) although the Reel was READY_FOR_REVIEW in the database 20 seconds after the click; a reload showed it. Cause: `ReviewPoller` called `router.refresh()` every 3 seconds while something was rendering, and the review page takes about 3.5 seconds to render against the remote database, so every refresh was replaced by the next before it arrived and none was shown (it worked now and then by luck). Fix: the refresh runs in a transition and a tick that finds the last refresh still pending is skipped (`review-poller.test.tsx`, 5 tests). A real deployment with a slow page would have shown the same freeze while a Reel or a carousel render was in progress.
- Not done: the spec is not in CI (it needs ffmpeg, Chrome and the dev database, like the other E2E specs); the review page is slow to render (about 3.5 s here): it makes a dozen queries per market, worth a look when the page is next touched.
- Evidence: the E2E spec passed; before the poller fix it failed twice at "ready"; unit tests for `scriptedReelScript`, `createE2eVideo` (playable portrait h264, one look per shot, the same clip for the same shot), `ReelsSwitch` (on, off, no button for a non-owner, Russian) and the poller; the i18n parity test; afterwards the database held no E2E rows, `reels.enabled` unchanged, both real Reels untouched.
- Impact on plan: M8-07a done.

## 2026-10-15 · Analytics collection: schema, Insights client, snapshot jobs; V-10 and V-11 [M6-01, M6-02, M6-03]
- Context: nothing told us how a post performed. The plan (11, 09 §9.5, 06 J11 and J12) wants snapshots with their real age, rates per reach compared at D7, and account metrics per day.
- V-10 and V-11, read from Meta's docs on 2026-10-15 (Insights guide, the media and user insights references, the changelog, the rate-limit pages): the metric list depends on `media_product_type`. FEED (a carousel parent, inferred; the docs have no CAROUSEL column): `reach`, `views`, `likes`, `comments`, `saved`, `shares`, `total_interactions`, `follows`, `profile_visits`, `profile_activity` (breakdown `action_type`). REELS: the common ones plus `ig_reels_avg_watch_time`, `ig_reels_video_view_total_time`, `reels_skip_rate`. `impressions` is an error for media after 2 July 2024 and since 21 April 2025 for everything, `plays` and `video_views` are gone, `facebook_views` and `crossposted_views` throw when the media is not on Facebook, `total_*` are Facebook Login only. Media metrics are lifetime (no `period`), come back as an empty data set instead of 0, can lag 48 hours and are kept 2 years. Account metrics: `period=day`, `metric_type=total_value`, `since` and `until` in Unix seconds (a breakdown needs `total_value`), `reach` by `follow_type` (`FOLLOWER`, `NON_FOLLOWER`, `UNKNOWN`), `views`, `accounts_engaged`, `total_interactions`, `follows_and_unfollows` (100 followers), kept 90 days; `followers_count` comes from the profile. Rate limits are business-use-case limits (`4800 × impressions` calls a day), error 80002.
- **Not documented and not yet verified live (V-10, V-11 stay open for the first live run):** the error for a metric a media type does not support (taken to be code 100 with the metric in the text, from third-party reports), whether a carousel parent takes the FEED list, the day boundary of account metrics (the examples suggest Pacific time; the code asks for the market's local day as an explicit window), the maximum `since`/`until` range, and which side of the `follow_type` split of `follows_and_unfollows` is follows (read as FOLLOWER = follows). The code survives each of them: a refused metric is dropped and cached, each call stands alone, an empty answer is null.
- Decision: (1) Migration `0011_analytics` (the tables of 04 §0007 and the three views by hand). Views differ from plan 11 in three ways: no `experiment_*` columns until 0012, `v_campaign_attribution` sums `quantity`, and equal distances to 168 h are broken by the newest measurement. (2) Snapshots are measurements with an age: a slot is collected when its time has come and no snapshot of the post is already as old as the slot; the latest such slot is measured. A post first looked at on day ten gets one snapshot named D7 (flagged late, true age kept) instead of four copies of the same numbers; a manual snapshot counts as a measurement of its age. (3) One call per media for the plain metrics and one for `profile_activity` (carousels); a Reel needs one. About 4 account calls a day per account. (4) Failures: one failing post is a finding on its account and does not stop the others; a refused token makes the account NEEDS_REAUTH and ends that account's posts; a rate limit stops the run and is rethrown for the backoff; an account's finding is closed only by a run with no failure for it. (5) `collectMetricsNow` is a server action (owner and editor) that starts J11 with the MANUAL slot; the screen for it comes with M6-09. (6) Jobs `collect-insights` (hourly at :07) and `collect-account-insights` (daily at 02:17 UTC) in the `instagram-read` queue.
- Not done: M6-04 onward (the query layer, the dashboards, the published list). The jobs run against Instagram only after the account is reconnected (it is NEEDS_REAUTH since 2026-10-13) and the scope `instagram_business_manage_insights` was granted: the connect flow already asks for it.
- Evidence: view tests on fixture data with known answers (18), slot rules (9), the Insights client against the mock Graph API (13), the collection services (17); `pnpm check`.
- Impact on plan: M6-01..03 done; 04 §0007 and 09 §9.5 updated; V-10 and V-11 checked from the docs.

## 2026-10-15 · Analytics query layer, dashboards, published list and lineage [M6-04, M6-05, M6-06, M6-07, M6-09]
- Context: M6-01..03 collect numbers (PR #108); nobody could read them. This branch is stacked on #108 (base `feat/m6-analytics`; retarget to `main` after it merges).
- Decision: (1) The query layer reads the three views through typed `pgView().existing()` declarations, so the same code runs on postgres.js and PGlite. (2) Learning groups only by a fixed whitelist of dimensions; the SQL of each is code, the caller passes a literal, never a column name. (3) Medians, not means, of per-post rates; a sample below the minimum (setting `analytics.min_sample`, default 3) is flagged, not hidden; lift is against the market median of the same range. (4) D7 is the snapshot closest to 168 h among those at least 72 h old; a D3 fallback is shown to the reader ("numbers at day 3"), never passed off as D7. (5) A metric Instagram did not return is a dash, never a zero, in tiles, tables and CSV. (6) The CSV export prefixes cells that start with a formula character, because hooks are written by a model and a spreadsheet would run `=HYPERLINK(...)`. (7) Charts follow the dataviz rules: one series per line chart, series colours validated (light #2a78d6 / #eb6834, dark #3987e5 / #d95926), a table view next to every chart, tooltips only as an extra. (8) The Reel card lives on the lineage screen of its carousel (answers the M8-07 open item); a Reel's lineage links back to the carousel's review page.
- Not done: M6-08 (attribution and the Commercial tab, P1), M6-10, M8-10 (Reel insights show in the numbers only as far as the shared columns go). The pages need migration 0011 applied; nothing can be shown from the real database until @chef.skk is reconnected and a collection has run. The review page still takes about 3.5 s against the remote database.
- Evidence: 30 query tests, 35 component tests, `pnpm check`; the dev preview at `/dev/analytics` looked at in the browser.
- Impact on plan: M6-04, 05, 06, 07, 09 done.

## 2026-10-15 · Progress of long actions on the review screen [M6-10]
- Context: on the first live "Regenerate all" (2026-10-12) the page showed nothing for about five minutes. The pipeline already writes where it is (`pipeline_state`, `visual_assets`, `carousel_renders`); nothing showed it.
- Decision: (1) Steps, not a percentage, read from the database in one query per idea, so a reload or a second tab shows the same. (2) One poll for the page (the provider), not one per panel; it slows down (3 s, 5 s, 10 s), does not run while the tab is hidden and ends when nothing runs, then the page is refreshed once; a refresh is never started while the last one is on its way (the freeze found in M8-07a). (3) "Taking longer than usual" is decided on the server from the last movement (`pipeline_state.updatedAt`, new and optional), so it also holds after a reload. (4) The usual time is the median of the last 20 runs, shown only from three runs on, so a made-up number is never shown. (5) The header indicator counts drafts and renders, ignores a run that began more than 2 h ago, and polls only while the count is above zero. (6) The helpers shared by the server page and the client (`isActive`, `pollDelay`) live in `lib/progress-model.ts`: the first build failed at run time ("Attempted to call isActive() from the server") because they were exported from a `"use client"` file; unit tests do not see that boundary, the E2E drafts spec did.
- Not done: see the task's entry in plan 15 (Reel card, publication panel, ideas and sources keep their own progress; a text rewrite shows as the next step for a moment).
- Evidence: 16 service tests on a database, 22 component tests, E2E `drafts` passes on the production build; `review` passes up to the calendar step and then fails on an unrelated expectation (its dialog now says "No Instagram account is connected for this market" because a real account is connected in the dev database; it expected "Publishing is switched off.").
- Impact on plan: M6-10 done; M6 has only M6-08 (P1) left.

## 2026-10-15 · Leads, sales and the Commercial tab [M6-08]
- Context: campaign ids exist since M4-11, but no lead or sale could be recorded, so revenue per post was unknown.
- Decision: (1) An event's market is the market of the post that has its campaign id, else the market given, else the id's market part (`rc-es-…`); a row with none of them is refused, not guessed. (2) Revenue is a sum per currency and is never added across currencies (EUR and USD markets). (3) Revenue per 1,000 reach is computed over the campaigns whose posts have D7 reach only; the others would flatter it. (4) The import reads only the listed columns, so the names and e-mails a shop export carries are never read; an order id that looks like an e-mail or phone is hashed (A-10). (5) A row without an order id gets a hash of its row number and content: importing the same file twice adds nothing, and two equal rows of one file both count. (6) The import report lives in the audit log (`attribution.import_requested`, `attribution.imported`); no table was added for it. (7) An event with an unknown campaign id is stored and listed for reconciliation rather than refused, so the money is never lost.
- Evidence: 14 module tests, 6 component tests, `pnpm check`.
- Impact on plan: M6-08 done; M6 is complete.

## 2026-10-15 · Performance memory: summary, J15, learning-aware prompts not active yet [M7-01, M7-02, M7-03, M7-04]
- Context: M7 makes generation learn from the D7 numbers. The plan names the prompts `idea-generator@2` and `content-writer@2`; those numbers were used up by later work (the independent-account versions, the hook library), so the new versions are idea-generator@3 and content-writer@6.
- Decision: (1) One summary per market and one across markets (`market_id` null, marketCode `ALL`); ideas are market-neutral, so the idea stage reads the one across markets, the writer reads its market's. Across markets every post's rate is divided by its own market's median before grouping, so a larger market does not set the patterns. (2) Pattern lists stay empty below `learning.min_posts_for_patterns` (default 20) D7-measured posts, with a caveat; overuse, gaps, offers and feedback are used from the first day. (3) Content reads the newest `performance_summaries` row itself (content may not import analytics); J15 runs in the J4 handler (the composition root) when the summary is more than 7 days old, and its failure never blocks ideas. (4) A summary older than 30 days is not given to a prompt. (5) Whether the memory is used follows the active prompt version, so activation is one config change. (6) **Not activated**: no post has D7 numbers in the shared database yet (@chef.skk needs a reconnect, no collection has run), so v2/v3 and v5/v6 would get the same input and an eval would prove nothing; the live comparison costs about $2.5 and needs the owner's yes. Activate after about 20 D7-measured posts per market: run `pnpm eval --provider live` with and without `--stage-version CONTENT_WRITING=6 --stage-version IDEA_GENERATION=3`, then switch both `STAGE_CONFIG` entries and set `PIPELINE_VERSION` 1.8.0 if nothing regresses.
- Evidence: schema, builder, job and prompt tests; `pnpm eval --provider fake` green with the active versions and with the new ones.
- Impact on plan: M7-01..04 done (M7-04 with activation pending); migration 0012 must be applied before deploy.


## 2026-10-15 · Dependency upgrades from the first audit; braces accepted [H-06a]
- Context: the first `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high` (H-06) found 6 high advisories: `next` < 16.3.8 (image-optimization SSRF), `sharp` < 0.35.5 (librsvg), `ws` < 8.21.0 (memory exhaustion), `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` < 1.31.0 (OAuth credentials sent to a server-chosen host), `source-map-js` < 1.2.2 (event-loop DoS) and `braces` ≤ 3.0.3 (stack exhaustion from nested patterns, no fixed version).
- Decision: (1) `next` 16.3.6 → 16.3.8, the smallest fixed patch; `@sentry/nextjs` stays at 11.0.0, its peer range already covers Next 16. (2) `shadcn` moves from `dependencies` to `devDependencies` of `@rc/web`: the app uses it only through `@import "shadcn/tailwind.css"` in `globals.css`, which Tailwind inlines at build time, and the CLI is for adding components by hand. That takes `braces`, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` and one `source-map-js` path out of production. (3) pnpm `overrides` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` for transitive versions: `ws` ^8.21.0 (from `@trigger.dev/core` > `socket.io-client` > `engine.io-client`, which pins ~8.17.1; same major, no API change), `sharp` ^0.35.5 and `source-map-js` ^1.2.2 (from `next` and `postcss`, the lockfile held the old patches), `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` ^1.31.0 (dev only now, still lifted). (4) **`braces` stays, accepted**: it is reached only by `shadcn` (ts-morph, fast-glob, micromatch) and the `trigger.dev` CLI (chokidar, micromatch), both dev tools run by hand or in deploy on patterns from their own config; no production code imports it, and no request, upload or form value reaches a glob or brace expansion. Revisit if a runtime package starts depending on it.
- Evidence: `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high` reports no high advisories (1 moderate left: `sprintf-js` via `mammoth` > `argparse`, no fix, below the threshold). `pnpm check`, `pnpm --filter @rc/web build` green. Resolved versions: next 16.3.8, sharp 0.35.5, ws 8.22.0, source-map-js 1.2.2, @modelcontextprotocol/sdk 1.32.1. The full audit (dev included) still lists `tar` (critical) and `deepmerge-ts` under the `trigger.dev` CLI and `@trigger.dev/build`, plus `braces`: dev tooling, outside H-06a.
- Impact on plan: H-06a done; the production audit in CI stops warning.

## 2026-10-16 · The Reel's own post: reel-director@2 [G-01]
- Context: the first two live Reels (2026-10-15) went out with their carousel's caption, so a video said "Swipe through 👉"; every post of the account asked for a save and carried the same hashtag block (#cookingtips on 8 of 8, #mealprep on 7); both Reels had the flash opener off although the accepted reference Reel had it on; 63 % and 81 % of the viewers left within three seconds (reach 16 and 30, a tiny sample). The review card did not show a Reel's caption at all, and a Reel variant could not be edited (it has no slides).
- Decision: (1) reel-director@2 = v1 plus the post, in the same call (no new stage, no new cost beyond output tokens): "caption" without its first line, "cta" from the codes the input allows, 3–5 "hashtags". (2) The first line is written by code from the chosen hook (card, then rule card), so "change hook" keeps the caption true; a first line the reviewer rewrote is left alone. (3) A Reel reaches people who do not follow the account, so the prompt prefers SHARE or FOLLOW and avoids the market's latest asks (from the last 8 approved or posted variants); a carousel ask tied to a keyword, an offer or a link is the only code offered and is kept. (4) Hashtags may reuse at most 2 of the recent tags (a MAJOR note, not a refusal: tags are taste, repairs cost). (5) Blocking checks: carousel words and arrows (en, es), engagement bait, tags or links in the caption, a number the carousel does not say, the ask not ending the caption, hashtag count and format, the market's forbidden patterns. (6) Flash opener ON by default; a hook that doubts a habit is recommended when the slides support it (the owner's rule of 2026-10-13). (7) A Reel's caption, ask and hashtags are edited by hand on the Reel card (no ↻: the field regenerator writes carousels); its hook and cards change only through "change hook". (8) `PIPELINE_VERSION` 1.8.0; content-writer@6 and idea-generator@3 stay inactive (M7-04 waits for D7 data).
- Evidence: prompt snapshot and tests, 9 validator tests, caption and hook-swap tests, generate test (the variant gets the Reel's caption, SHARE and its tags), Reel card test (block shown, edit sent to the Reel variant), `pnpm eval:reels --provider fake` 3/3 with the post on the review sheet, `pnpm check` green.
- Live eval (owner's yes, 2026-10-16, `pnpm eval:reels --provider live`, claude-opus-5-5): 3/3 valid scripts, no repair, 0 notes, $0.31, 122 s. All three recommended a doubt-about-a-habit hook ("¿Arroz lavado para risotto?", "Just add hot water to bulgur?", "Still oiling your pasta water?") with the flash opener ON; every caption retells only the carousel (numbers 2 min and 10 min as on the slides), ends with a SHARE ask naming the person ("Send this to whoever makes the bulgur at home"), no carousel words; hashtags reuse at most 2 recent tags. All three chose SHARE (the inputs' recent asks were SAVE and FOLLOW): watch on real posts that FOLLOW also comes up once SHARE is among the recent asks.
- Not done: carousel asks and hashtags are G-04.
- Impact on plan: new section G in plan 15 (growth); G-01 done.

## 2026-10-16 · Alt text goes to Instagram [G-02, V-30]
- Context: the writer gives every slide an `altText` and the approved snapshot keeps it, but the publish machine never sent it, so Instagram had no description of our images (search and screen readers read it).
- Decision: send the snapshot's alt text of each slide as `alt_text` of its carousel child container: whitespace folded, cut to Meta's 1,000 characters, left out when empty. The carousel parent and Reels get none (Meta takes it on images only). What is sent is what the reviewer approved; nothing is generated at publish time.
- Evidence: publish-machine test (two slides with alt text, one without, the long one cut, none on the parent); Meta's IG User Media reference read 2026-10-16.
- Not done: V-30, the first dry run with alt text through `graph.instagram.com`.
- Impact on plan: G-02 code done.

## 2026-10-16 · Account metrics are read after each market's own night [G-03]
- Context: J12 ran once a day at 02:17 UTC and took "yesterday" in the market's zone. At 02:17 UTC it is still 22:17 the day before in New York, so the first run (2026-10-16) stored 14 Oct for the English account; 15 Oct, the day of the first Reels, would have arrived only on 17 Oct, a day and a half late. The first row also showed `follows_and_unfollows` empty (follows and unfollows null) for an account with 2 followers (V-11, to watch).
- Decision: J12 runs hourly at :17. On a scheduled run an account is read once its market's local clock has passed 02:00 (the day has ended and Meta has had two hours to add it up) and only if that day is not stored yet; a run with one account id reads it now and overwrites the day, as before. In steady state each market is read once a day, about 02:17 local (00:17 UTC for Madrid in summer, 06:17 UTC for New York); a missed day is caught up on the next run. Later runs of the day only read the database, so Meta sees no more calls than before.
- Evidence: collect tests (the window per zone, Madrid read at 02:17 UTC and New York at 06:17 UTC, "too early" at 00:30 local, a stored day skipped with no Graph call, one account overwritten on request).
- Impact on plan: G-03 schedule fixed; plan 06 J12 row updated. V-11 stays open until follows and unfollows are seen on a real account.

## 2026-10-17 · Photo placed by its subject; pictures with a meaning (visual-director@7) [CV-01, CV-02]
- Context: the owner compared the English carousels with the Russian-language reference account (docs/design/carousel-v3-requirements.md). Ours: one black pot of pale grain on most slides, subjects cut into fragments, a skillet step showing a pot, text under a photo and a headline broken inside a word. The cause of the fragments was in the code: the photo prompt put the subject low and right, and every template moved the picture again by a fixed offset (up to +330/+400 px).
- Decision: (1) CV-01: a placement script in the slide page measures the subject on the white and fits it beside the text, as large as the text allows (templates 2.2.0); off-white pictures are lifted to white; display slots shrink instead of breaking a word; render QA blocks a picture that touches text and warns when less than 85 % of a subject is on the slide. Cut-outs are normalized with white padding instead of an attention crop, so a whole bowl at the top of a 2:3 frame stays whole. (2) CV-02: visual-director@7 (active, `PIPELINE_VERSION` 1.9.0): one moment per slide quoted in `depicts`, the same food in a new state each time, whole subjects, white porcelain, steel and glass with a dark vessel at most once, colour from food, comparisons as two servings, arrangements MISE_EN_PLACE, HERO_WITH_DEPTH and TOOL_IN_ACTION; briefs with a `vessel` get whole-subject compositions and their own style and negative list; the validator checks `depicts` against the slide, dark vessels, repeated pictures and four arrangements from six pictures on.
- Evidence: `pnpm check` green; browser tests for placement and word breaks; 15 validator and prompt tests. Live check on two published English carousels (the owner asked on 2026-10-17 to keep improving the posts overnight without waiting for him; spend capped at $5): wet rice ($0.56: director $0.14 + 7 pictures, a second director run $0.12) and buckwheat ($0.36: director $0.11 + 6 pictures), rendered with `bench:carousel`. Both briefs passed without repair; the pictures show steel pans, white porcelain bowls, a burner, a lid, three spoons of rice in three states, the fats laid out around a bowl, a spoon lifting dry grains from a steel pot. One of three director runs repeated a picture (blocked by the new check; the pipeline repairs it once).
- Not done: text density and the arc of the reference (CV-03, CV-04); the white cover (D1).
- Impact on plan: CV-01, CV-02 done.

## 2026-10-17 · Carousel v3 text: templates 3.0, market-adapter@4, content-writer@7 [CV-03, CV-04]
- Context: the reference account teaches a method in 8 slides (the usual way, the principle, the portion, steps with numbers, the check), one sentence per line, with amounts and times, a quieter lead and a bold closing line, and repeats the whole method in the caption. Ours carried 100–220 characters of general lines per slide and asked for a save.
- Decision: (1) Templates 3.0.0: ladder bodies at 26–30 px in a 468 px column, `lead` and `closer` slots, larger limits (B body 380, E 200 + 300, F 300, A subline 140); **the plan 08 floor of 32 px for body text is lowered to 26 px** (the reference uses 27–35 px on a 1080 px slide; the owner can veto). (2) White cover as a brand switch (`visual_system.cover`), default dark until the owner decides D1. (3) market-adapter@4 (the arc) and content-writer@7 (ladder, numbers, cues, no filler, lead and closer, sparing red, hook slide with what it delivers, sign-off line, full-method caption) active, `PIPELINE_VERSION` 1.10.0. content-writer@7 is built on v5, not v6: the learning of v6 waits for D7 numbers (2026-10-15), so `writerUsesLearning` reads only version 6. (4) `validateSubstance`: filler is MAJOR; a thin inner slide or a step without a number is a MINOR note only, so the closed book is never pushed to invent a number.
- Evidence: `pnpm check` green (2,885 tests); new tests for ladder gaps, lead and closer, step labels, the white cover, filler, thin slides, half-line gaps in the line estimate. Live run (the owner asked on 2026-10-17 to keep improving overnight without waiting; spend capped at $5, $2.24 used in total) on a private case made from the owner's own rice method (two cards with steps, amounts and times; outside git), English only: plan of 9 slides, writer without repair, critic PASS, one MAJOR line-count note (fixed by the half-line gap count), caption 1,288 characters with the numbered method and the "Important:" limit; $0.94 for the text, $0.38 for 9 pictures from the pipeline's own brief, rendered with `bench:carousel`.
- Finding: the approved knowledge is thin (81 cards, 22 approved, 20 with steps, 5 with timings, from one 18-page guide and two posts). The prompts cannot add amounts the cards lack: CV-07 (owner's sources) decides as much as the prompts.
- Not done: G-04 moves to the next writer version; CV-05 spike, CV-06 lead magnet, CV-07 knowledge, CV-08 Reels.
- Impact on plan: CV-03, CV-04 done; CV-07, CV-08 added.

## 2026-10-17 · Slot lines are counted at the smallest size; the new prompts on thin knowledge [CV-04]
- Context: both live v3 drafts got a TEXT_OVERFLOW flag (SLOT_LINES, "body needs about 15–16 lines, the limit is 13") although the render fitted them: the line estimate used the characters per line of the largest size, while fit-text shrinks a long text down to `minPx`, where the box holds more and longer lines.
- Decision: `validateSlideAgainstTemplate` counts lines against `slotAtSmallestSize` (the box's lines and characters per line scaled by `maxPx / minPx`); `SLOT_LINES` stays MAJOR. A ladder's blank line counts half a line (its drawn gap).
- Evidence: both live drafts pass; template tests (seven lines in A's headline still flagged; five long words that shrink to fit are not). Live run of the active pipeline on the real wet-rice idea (one bare claim, no numbers; $0.72): a correct 6-slide carousel without invented numbers, critic PASS, but slides 3–5 repeat the same two fixes. With the knowledge base as it is, the next real posts will look like this; CV-07 (the owner's sources) is what makes them look like the rice demo. Total live spend overnight: $2.97.

## 2026-10-17 · Hybrid edit spike: the image model does not keep to the mask [CV-05, D2]
- Context: D2 option C (render the final text slide, let the image model paint the dish into the empty zone with a mask, keep only the masked pixels) promised the reference's integrated composition with exact text.
- Evidence: one high 1024 × 1536 edit of the rice step-2 slide with gpt-image-2.5-sunburst, $0.054 from `usage`. The model's answer re-drew the whole slide: the dish was well placed beside the text, but the text was re-rendered (one body line squeezed onto one line) and the pot reached into the text area. Compositing only the masked pixels kept the text exact but cut the pot's left side and left a faint off-white block.
- Decision: C is dropped; A (measured placement, CV-01) stays the way slides are made. B (whole slide by the model) stays rejected for text control.

## 2026-10-17 · The Reel's grid cover is the carousel's cover slide [CV-08, R3]
- Context: Reels went out with their first frame as the grid cover, so the grid showed a bare pot between the carousels' text covers.
- Verification: Meta's IG User Media reference (read 2026-10-17) has `cover_url` for REELS: "The path to an image to use as the cover image for the Reels tab", fetched by Meta from a public URL; JPEG, at most 8 MB, sRGB; 9:16 recommended, otherwise Meta crops the middle 9:16 rectangle; `cover_url` wins over `thumb_offset`.
- Decision: at approval the source carousel's rendered first slide is placed in a 1080 × 1920 JPEG with bands in the slide's own top and bottom edge colours (a white cover gets white bands, a dark one dark bands; the grid's 3:4 crop shows the whole slide), stored next to the video and frozen in `snapshot.reel.cover` (key, SHA-256, bytes). The publish machine sends it as `cover_url` from a fresh presigned link when it is still in storage. Anything missing or failing leaves the first frame, as before; the cover never blocks an approval or a publish.
- Evidence: unit test of the cover image; Reel publishing tests (cover made, stored and sent as `cover_url`; no cover without a carousel render); `pnpm check` green.
- Open: the first Reel dry run with a cover on @chef.skk (V-26 showed Meta fetches a presigned R2 mp4; the JPEG should behave the same).

The theme default is now `cover: "white"` (the dark full-bleed cover stays as `visual_system.cover = "dark"`); the cover's picture is generated and normalized like an inner picture (whole subject, white padding) unless the brand asks for the dark cover. ## 2026-10-17 · An empty model account pauses generation; the top-up costs nothing twice [M2-13c]
- Context: the owner ran out of Anthropic credit in the middle of a batch. Anthropic answers 400 "credit balance is too low"; the adapter made it a `PermanentError`, `generateVariants` treated it like any non-transient error (`fail()` for every variant: `pipeline_state` null, `GENERATION_FAILED`), and the rerun repeated MARKET_ADAPTATION, CONTENT_WRITING and CRITIC that were already paid for (7 variants, about $1.66).
- Decision: the adapter maps 400 with that message (or `billing_error`, 402) to `CreditsExhaustedError`, a subclass of `PermanentError` so every existing handler (jobs abort without retry, extraction batches, shots) behaves as before. In `generateVariants` it is a pause: stages in flight are awaited and recorded (`settle` instead of `Promise.all`), then each live variant goes GENERATING → DRAFT with `pipeline_state` kept, `flags` empty, `lastError { code: AI_CREDITS_EXHAUSTED, details { stage, resumable } }` and the message "top up and generate again", audit `variant.generation_paused`; the error is rethrown. A later run (any run id, as every button makes a new one) finds a DRAFT with that error code and its state, adopts the state under its own `pipelineRunId` (so a transient retry of the new job passes the lock check), keeps the finished stage ids and restarts the clock; audit `variant.generation_resumed`. `orderSeed` (new optional `pipeline_state` field) keeps the shuffle of the first run, so resumed markets are still planned in the order their plans were made. A person's note changes the writer's input, so a run with one starts over. Reel job: same classification, `reel.paused`, result `PAUSED`, state kept on the DRAFT, no flag; its script, shots and render are found by their rows and hashes, and only the director calls the model, so a new request after the top-up repeats no paid work either.
- Not done: stored stage outputs are reused however long the pause was (a prompt version or card change in between is not detected; `KNOWLEDGE_CHANGED` still flags changed cards); the other jobs that call the model or the picture and video providers (M2-13d).
- Evidence: fake-provider tests (pause keeps ADAPT/WRITE/finished CRITIC, resume under a new run id makes only the cut-off review call, second empty account pauses again, a note starts over, other errors still wipe, Reel pause then success); adapter tests against MSW for the 400 text and the 402; `pnpm check` green.

## 2026-10-18 · Calm hook: the flash opener is off, the hook is held 2.5 s [G]
- Context: the first Reel with the flash opener (the chocolate myth Reel, reel-director@2, published 2026-10-18) kept viewers about 2 s on average by the owner's Instagram numbers; the three Reels before it, all without the opener, kept 4.3–5.0 s (D1, skip 70–85 %). Four 0.22 s cuts before the question read as noise: the viewer cannot tell what the Reel is about in the first second.
- Decision (owner): no flash opener; the hook scene lasts the longest a scene may (5 beats, 2.5 s) and its card shows from the first frame. `toReelScriptDraft` sets both whatever the director answers, so reel-director@2 stays active without a new prompt version; its text about "ON by default" is now ignored. Renderer 1.2.0 (the first card no longer fades in, so the input hash changes and a re-render is a new render).
- Not proved: one Reel against three, different topics. Next: compare the same Reel with and without the opener (Trial Reels to non-followers), and a 7–9 s loop.
